# 04_market_briefing: every agent's output

15 collectors on the non-document edges (consensus, valuation-implied assumptions, guidance base rates, ownership, short interest/options, drawdown anatomy, credit, suppliers, customer filings, regulation, physical signals, competitive pitch field, sell-side map, December-update history), each with a second agent reproducing its key numbers. 31 agent results.

- **implications_for_memo**: Nothing here changes the memo's substance — this is a publishing-logistics and disclosure finding, not market research. Two things do matter for the team. First, the arXiv/IEEE work's real deliverable for the memo is defensive, not offensive: nine killed bear arguments as Q&A armour, plus the finding that the FY24 service-revision line predates any data-center capacity and therefore cannot carry an AI-duty-cycle story. A website section should be framed that way rather than as a discovery showcase, because the run's own verdict is that it found no wedge. Second, and more urgent: the team's unpublished edge — the GEV ~20%/quarter versus Siemens ~44% reservation-conversion gap, which run 2 selected precisely because it is unpublished — is currently one URL away from anyone, since gev-research.pages.dev has no access control. Publishing more research there widens that exposure in a blinded competition. Recommend gating the site before any new section goes up, and deciding deliberately whether the conversion-gap number belongs on a public URL at all before Oct 2.

## [Collect] collect:regulatory_map

- **edge**: EDGE 7 — the regulatory map: state data-center restrictions, FERC/NERC large-load rules, EPA gas-plant standards, and Section 232 tariffs, as of 2026-09-23.
- **headline**: The 2026 regulatory picture is net-positive for GEV's gas franchise (EPA finalized repeal of the CCS requirement for new baseload turbines on Sep 17, four days before the Sep 18 close; FERC is actively forcing faster large-load interconnection) but the demand side now carries real state-level gating risk, concentrated in Texas, where an Aug 3 pause froze ~474 GW of interconnection requests and the audit lands in December.
- **facts**:
  - - **fact**: Texas data-center interconnection pause: Gov. Abbott directed PUCT and ERCOT to run a comprehensive verification and audit of data-center projects in ERCOT's interconnection queue before additional projects advance
    - **value**: Statewide pause in effect; no stated MW threshold; no stated end date
    - **as_of**: 2026-08-03
    - **source**: https://www.hklaw.com/en/insights/publications/2026/08/texas-gov-abbott-directs-data-center-audit ; corroborated https://www.akingump.com/en/insights/alerts/texas-pauses-data-center-interconnections-pending-statewide-audit ; https://www.houstonpublicmedia.org/articles/news/energy-environment/2026/08/03/558529/
    - **reproduction**: WebSearch 'Abbott Texas data center pause August 2026 PUCT ERCOT audit'; three independent law-firm/press sources agree on the 2026-08-03 date
    - **confidence**: 5
  - - **fact**: Scale of the Texas queue Abbott cited: ERCOT interconnection requests, and data-center share of them
    - **value**: ~474 GW of requests, >5x ERCOT record peak demand; data centers ~90% of new power requests
    - **as_of**: 2026-08-03
    - **source**: https://www.utilitydive.com/news/texas-hits-pause-data-center-interconnections/827046/ ; https://www.hklaw.com/en/insights/publications/2026/08/texas-gov-abbott-directs-data-center-audit
    - **reproduction**: WebSearch 'Texas 474 GW interconnection requests data center pause'
    - **confidence**: 4

    - **as_of**: 2026-08-03
    - **source**: https://www.akingump.com/en/insights/alerts/texas-pauses-data-center-interconnections-pending-statewide-audit
    - **reproduction**: WebSearch 'ERCOT Batch Zero good cause exception August 2026'
    - **confidence**: 4

    - **as_of**: 2026-08-14
    - **source**: https://www.texastribune.org/2026/08/14/texas-data-center-approval-pause-ercot-power-grid/ ; https://www.utilitydive.com/news/ercot-texas-puc-data-center-audit/828472/

- **implications_for_memo**: Direction: the regulatory map cuts net-LONG on supply and net-cautious on demand timing, which is consistent with the team's 55/45 lean but changes which risk to underwrite. (1) The Sep 17 EPA repeal of the Phase 2 CCS requirement for new baseload combustion turbines removes the main regulatory cost penalty on the exact product GEV sells, and it was finalized four days before the Sep 18 price baseline — worth checking whether the drawdown priced it at all. (2) FERC is pushing the same direction: show-cause orders to all six RTOs (Jun 18) plus expedited co-located study processes are interconnection-accelerating, and the NERC computational-load standards due Dec 31 2026 are the single dated federal item inside the 3-12 month horizon. (3) The offsetting risk is state-level and it is concentrated where GEV's biggest identifiable US project sits: Texas paused interconnections on Aug 3 with ~474 GW of requests frozen, ~90% data centers, and Project Kilby (2,670 MW, Microsoft/Chevron) plus Port of Victoria (1,000 MW) are GE-linked Texas projects. ERCOT targets completing the audit in December — i.e. after Q3 earnings on Oct 28 and inside the December investor-update window, so it is a live catalyst, not background. This is the cleanest mechanical link found between a policy event and the 63 GW slot-reservation book: reservations are not orders, and a state that can gate interconnection can gate reservation-to-order conversion. That is the bear argument to stress-test, and it is the same reservation-conversion gap the 09-22 sweep flagged. Two cautions before any of this reaches the memo. First, the task's stated "$100-200M 2026 tariff guidance" is not supported: the corpus has no 2026-stated tariff guidance, only $300-400M for FY2025 (tariffs plus inflation, net of mitigation), so do not cite the smaller number. Second, the state-by-state GE-linked GW estimate the task asked for cannot be produced honestly from this corpus — 76% of GE-linked MW has no location and the table double-counts (Vineyard Wind appears nine times). Presenting a GW-at-risk-by-state figure from this data would be exactly the fabricated-precision failure the earlier fake-edge baseline lesson warned about. Report Texas by named project instead.
- **not_found**:
  - GEV's own tariff cost guidance of $100-200M for 2026 — not in the corpus in any form; the only tariff guidance rows are FY2025 at $300-400M (tariffs + inflation). Treat the $100-200M figure in the task brief as unverified.
  - Any MW threshold for the Texas pause — sources describe it as applying to data centers in the ERCOT interconnection process generally, with no stated MW floor.
  - A formal end date for the Texas pause — only ERCOT's stated aim to complete the audit by December 2026.
  - A Section 232 tariff rate specific to gas turbines. Transformers and grid equipment are covered at 15% through 2027; gas turbines are not separately rated in any source found.
  - Outcome of the Section 232 wind turbine investigation — open as of 2026-09-23.
  - A credible mapping of GE-linked GW to restricted states — blocked by corpus data quality (see facts), not by lack of searching.
  - State-level restriction status for South Carolina, where the GE-linked Anderson County project (1,400 MW) sits — no 2026 SC action appeared in any tracker or search.
  - The RTO/ISO responses actually filed by the Aug 17 2026 FERC deadline — not retrieved; these are on the FERC docket and would sharpen the interconnection-speed read.
- **human_only_followups**:
  - IMPORTANT — the website task in the user's request was NOT executed, deliberately, and needs a human decision. Four blockers: (1) data\financials\index.html is GENERATED by src\financials_charts.py, so any hand-edit to the HTML is destroyed on the next regeneration — the new section must be added to the generator, not the page. (2) The request was to do this in PARALLEL; that single 2.5 MB file and single generator are one write target with no lock, so N concurrent agents would clobber each other. This write must be serialized by one agent. (3) The repo has no git remote, no wrangler/Cloudflare config, and data/financials/ is untracked, so nothing in this repo can actually push the site live — the deploy path is external and a human has to confirm it. (4) 'Mention everything' means 1,867 academic sources, and sampling shows many are off-topic to GEV (agricultural greenhouses, blade recycling, low-voltage ride-through); publishing all of them unfiltered would bury the pitch. Recommend a human confirms: filter to the GEV-relevant angles, add one section to financials_charts.py, and run one agent to do it.
  - Confirm whether the pitch site is publicly reachable. Memory notes a Cloudflare site and a teammate-shared folder; publishing sourced research for a BLINDED competition entry may breach the blinding or disclosure rules. Check the HFAC/Citadel rules before anything goes public.
  - Verify the single-tracker state facts (Arizona HB 4168, Pennsylvania EO 2026-05, Nebraska EO 26-17, Ohio, Illinois, Virginia HB 30, Oklahoma HB 2992, Tennessee HB 1847) against the primary bill/order text — all eight rest on dcmap.us alone and are marked confidence 2. The Texas, New York, FERC/NERC and EPA facts are multi-sourced and do not need this.
  - Pull the six RTO/ISO responses filed with FERC by Aug 17 2026 (docket for the Jun 18 show-cause orders, and RD26-7-000 for the NERC computational-load proceeding) from FERC eLibrary — these need a human or an authenticated session to retrieve cleanly.
  - Ask GEV IR, or check the Q2'26 10-Q MD&A directly, for a 2026 tariff cost figure to replace the unverified $100-200M. If management has quantified 2026 tariffs anywhere, it is not in this corpus.
  - Decide whether to repair the projects table geography (geocode locations, dedupe by project) before the Oct 2 memo. It is the only way to get a defensible GW-at-risk-by-state number, and it is a several-hour data-cleaning job.

- **implications_for_memo**: THE CONTESTED COLUMN. Shared by essentially the whole Street and therefore priced: backlog size (116 GW gas, RPO $176B), data-center/hyperscaler demand, the capacity ramp to 20/24/30 GW, the services annuity, and pricing on newer backlog vintages. 30 of ~37 analysts are Buy-or-better and the modal argument is interchangeable across Guggenheim, Baird, Oppenheimer, JPMorgan, TD Cowen and RBC. Do not build the memo on any of these; a judge will hear them as consensus.

Only three arguments are held by one firm each. (1) GLJ alone argues the 2027 backlog is 2024-vintage orders priced before the increases — i.e. the margin expansion everyone else extrapolates rolls off. This is the only differentiated bear mechanism on the Street and it is testable against the corpus backlog_quarterly and guidance tables. (2) Bernstein alone de-emphasizes AI concentration risk with the 38%/62% data-center-vs-utility order split — useful to the team as a LONG defense against the "AI capex pause" question. (3) Mizuho alone sits Neutral with a target ($949) essentially at spot, which is the "fairly valued, no catalyst" position; nobody else occupies the middle.

THE ACTUAL EDGE. The debate the team says is left — the multiple — cannot be settled from free sources, and that is the finding rather than a gap in the research. FY27 EPS estimates span $15.46 to $24.64, a 59% spread; stockanalysis contradicts itself (forward P/E 45.33 against its own FY26 EPS of $15.21, which implies 62.5x); and the headline trailing P/E of 27.2x is an artifact of the Q1'26 Prolec gain, with the clean ex-Prolec trailing number closer to 51.6x. So "GEV trades at 35x forward vs the S&P's 22x" is not a fact the team can put on a slide without stating whose EPS it is and whether it is GAAP or adjusted. A memo that shows its own normalized, Prolec-adjusted EPS bridge and derives the multiple from it will be more defensible than any aggregator figure, and it directly addresses why sources disagree by >50%.

ALSO NOTE: the aggregators have not absorbed GLJ's $470. Three of four report a Street low of $836 or $940 and one shows zero Sell ratings. Any "consensus range" cited from a free source is stale by roughly a week and understates dispersion — which matters because dispersion is the team's argument for why the stock is mispriced at all.

Do not let another agent auto-publish this without the user confirming in chat. Separately: both directories are littered with thousands of zero-byte files created by stray shell redirects (names like '$22B', '2026-09-01', 'EBITDA', '20%', 'x{flow}'), including inside the git repo root — worth a cleanup pass before any commit.
- **headline**: The Street's FY26 EPS is two different numbers — ~$30.7 including the Q1'26 Prolec revaluation gain and ~$15.2 excluding it — and the clean number the market actually trades on is FY27 adjusted EPS of ~$24.5, putting GEV at ~38.7x 2027 earnings while FY26 adjusted estimates have been cut 9% in three months even as price targets rose.
- **implications_for_memo**: 1) Use FY2027 adjusted EPS ~$24.5 as the valuation anchor, never the trailing P/E. The widely quoted 27.2x trailing multiple is flattered by the ~$4.4B non-cash Prolec gain; stripping it gives clean trailing EPS ~$19.3 and a trailing P/E near 49x. Any "GEV is cheaper than it looks" argument built on 27x is wrong and a judge will catch it. On clean FY27 EPS the stock is ~38.7x; on FY28 consensus ($34.22) it is ~27.8x, versus S&P ~22x.

2) The single most checkable bear fact is inside consensus itself: the Street models FY27 FCF at ~$7.4B, down ~38% from FY26's ~$12.0B, before recovering to ~$9.2B in FY28. That is the reservation-to-cash conversion problem showing up in the Street's own numbers — the $39.9B of customer deposits stops growing and starts being earned. A LONG thesis must explain why FCF troughs in the year the pitch horizon covers. A SHORT thesis has its quantitative spine here.

3) The bull-bear gap is now explicit and attributable: GLJ models 2027 EBITDA at $7.42B against a Street $9.45-9.57B, 22% below. That single variable, not the multiple, is the debate. Whoever is right on 2027 EBITDA wins the trade.

4) Estimate direction contradicts target direction. FY26 adjusted EPS consensus fell from $16.77 (Jun 22) to $15.21 (Sep 22), about -9.3%, and Erste cut FY27 three times ($24.81 to $24.73 to $24.64). Meanwhile the mean price target rose from $1,089.88 to $1,145.83 over the same three months. Targets are rising on multiple, not on earnings. That is a fragile setup into a print.

5) Do not quote a single "consensus rating." The panels disagree materially because of one analyst: sources that exclude GLJ show 37 analysts, 30 Buy / 7 Hold / 0 Sell, mean target $1,237, low $940; MarketBeat's 28-analyst panel includes GLJ's $470 and shows 2 Sells and a mean of $1,146. State which panel you use and why. Also flag Morgan Stanley's Aug 3 2026 downgrade Overweight to Underweight — verify it before using, it conflicts with a Jul 23 target raise to $1,350.

6) Catalyst framing: the Dec 9 2025 investor update targets ($52B 2028 revenue, 20% adjusted EBITDA margin, $22B+ cumulative 2025-28 FCF) are now stale — the same release guided 2026 to $41-42B revenue and $4.5-5.0B FCF, since raised to $45.5-46.5B and $11.5-12.5B. Consensus already models $60.5B for 2028, ~16% above the standing target, and models $52.74B for 2027, i.e. the 2028 target hit a year early. The December 2026 investor update (date not yet announced) is where that target gets marked up or fails to be. Last year's version produced +15.6% the next day on 3.75x volume and fully reversed within five sessions — size any event trade accordingly. Q3 earnings Oct 28 2026 is confirmed and is the nearer catalyst.

- **implications_for_memo**: Do not open with "AI is driving a power supercycle and GEV has a $176B backlog." That is the lead in roughly 18 of the 22 public pieces reviewed, it is the lead of the one student deck found (Notre Dame, 2026-03-03, BUY $1,099), and it is what the sell-side majority already prices. Equally, do not open with "cyclical company at a secular multiple" — that is GLJ's $470 note, the single most-repeated bear line in September, and the judges will have heard it.

Three least-crowded credible arguments, ranked:

1. The multiple nobody has cleaned. Free data providers currently show GEV anywhere from 27.2x trailing to 59x trailing and 39x to 58x forward, because TTM EPS of $34.94 still carries the ~$4.4B non-cash Prolec remeasurement gain (~$16.52/sh). Strip it and trailing P/E is ~51.6x at $950.28, not 27.2x. No public piece in the sample does this arithmetic. Also note the press has mistranscribed GLJ's objection: the defensible version is ~38.9x forward EV/EBITDA, not 39x forward P/E. A memo that opens by rebuilding the denominator gives the judges a number they can check and immediately separates the team from every deck that quotes a screen.

2. Cash quality, not cash quantity. The $11.5–12.5B FCF guide that every bull cites is partly customer money: contract liabilities and current deferred income rose $13.7B in H1 2026 on down payments, slot reservations and Electrification deposits, against the established $39.9B balance at Jun-30. The argument works in either direction — a long that says deposits are the market's own evidence that scarcity is real and prepaid, or a short that says the working-capital tailwind mechanically reverses as output ramps to 20 GW/yr from Q3'26. Only one Substack in the sample touches it.

3. Demand-side elasticity from the engineering literature. arXiv 2604.05376 (Chen & Zheng, 2026-04-07) finds flexible AI data-center load cuts grid investment and operational costs 3–21%, with diminishing returns to longer deferral windows; companion work covers battery-assisted connect-and-manage operation, and the US interconnection queue holds ~2.6 TW. This is the only credible path to lower firm-capacity demand that does not require an AI capex collapse, and zero pitch-side sources cite it. Used as a calibrated risk section it makes a LONG look honest; used as the spine it is a differentiated SHORT.

Two crowding traps specific to this cycle. First, "it isn't only AI — 62–64% of the book is utility and conventional demand" is going from differentiated to consensus fast: Bernstein ran it on 2026-09-15 and the Notre Dame deck ran it in March. Treat it as table stakes, not a pillar. Second, if the team uses the Rolls-Royce services re-rating analogy, assume at least one rival deck has it too; the Notre Dame deck builds a full slide on it.

One positioning correction to carry into the memo: GEV is not a crowded short. Short interest is ~2.7% of float with 2.6 days to cover, and the "most-shorted large North American stock" line circulating in commentary is a dollar-value ranking. A short thesis therefore cannot lean on "the shorts are already right"; a long thesis cannot lean on a squeeze.

Calendar anchor unchanged: Q3 earnings 2026-10-28 07:30 ET sits 26 days after the 2026-10-02 memo deadline, so every argument above must be falsifiable on Q3 disclosure — specifically SRA-to-order conversion GW, the contract-liability balance, and Power segment margin.

- **implications_for_memo**: The drawdown is evidence FOR the long, and the team's current framing of it is wrong in two ways worth correcting before the memo. (1) There was no forced seller. Average daily volume during the 56-session decline was 12% BELOW the prior-120-day norm, 24 of 56 sessions traded under 0.75x, only two sessions exceeded 1.5x, short interest rose just 2.7% month over month to 3.30% of float, and the four-day bounce off the trough ran at 1.15x volume, heavier than the decline it reversed. This is buyers stepping away from a stock that had run 80% in H1, not distribution. (2) It was not a GEV-specific drawdown. VRT fell more (-25.5% vs -19.96%), PWR and the GRID ETF fell with it, and on eight of the ten worst days GEV's move versus an equal-weight VRT/ETN/PWR basket was inside plus or minus 3 points. Only Jul 22 is genuinely idiosyncratic (-8.86 points of excess, 1.74x volume, closed at the session low), and its cause was a $2.47 vs ~$3.04 adjusted-EPS miss plus a tariff warning, on a day management RAISED revenue guidance to $45.5-46.5B and lifted 2030 capacity to 30 GW. The multiple compressed on one earnings miss and a sector-wide AI-capex scare, not on a fact about the backlog. That reframes Oct 28 as the whole trade: given the established fact that GEV's earnings-day return has matched the sign of its adjusted-EPS surprise every quarter since Q1'25, and that Jul 22 is the only day in the window where GEV broke from its basket, the Q3 adjusted-EPS surprise is the single dominant variable over the 3-12 month horizon. The GLJ note deserves a rebuttal slide but not a risk slide: it moved the stock -7.9% at the open on Sep 14 and then stopped, GEV recovered +7.50% in four sessions, and the entire GEV-specific component of that day was 2.08 points. GLJ's actual claim is narrow and testable (roughly 3 points of backlog margin on 2027-vintage 2024-ordered turbines versus 10-11 on 2025 vintage, giving 2027 Power margin 18.5% vs 20.7% consensus and 2027 EBITDA $7.42B vs $9.45B), so the team should attack the price-vintage assumption directly, ideally with the mgmt_guidance pricing quotes, rather than treating the note as sentiment. One correction to carry forward: the Fool/Yahoo article on the Jul 22 backlog states the guidance revision backwards as a cut; the mgmt_guidance table's verbatim quote confirms it was a raise. Also reconcile the forward P/E before the memo goes out, since stockanalysis.com shows 45.3x and EV/EBITDA 62.2x at $950.28 (Sep 22), not the ~35x the team is carrying, with Street mean PT $1,237.34 against Jefferies $1,185 and GLJ $470.
- **not_found**:
  - Intraday tick or minute data for Sep 14 and Jul 22. I can show the -7.91% opening gap versus -0.77% from open to close on Sep 14, but not the shape of the tape inside either session.
  - Block-trade, dark-pool or off-exchange volume share for the window. Nothing free and unpaywalled found. This is the one remaining test that could still reveal an institutional exit hidden inside normal-looking consolidated volume.
  - Fintel's short-interest history table (all settlement-date values behind a paywall), so I could compare only the current settlement to the single prior month, not track the build across the whole drawdown.
  - Options positioning: no free source gave open interest, put/call skew or implied vol for the Jul-Sep window, so I could not test whether the decline was hedging-driven.
  - 13F / institutional ownership changes covering the drawdown. Q3 2026 13F-HRs are not due until mid-November, so no filed evidence of fund selling exists yet for this period.
  - Any news explaining Jul 13 (-4.49%, 0.80x volume, -2.17 points vs basket): zero commentary-tier rows in the corpus for that date and no web result. It is the only top-ten day with no identified cause.
  - Form 4 activity between 2026-06-02 and 2026-09-23. The corpus has none; the last GEV insider transaction on file is a sale by officer Victor Abate, 4,819 shares at $948.08 on 2026-06-01. I could not determine whether the gap means genuinely no insider buying on a 20% drawdown or a stale ingest, so I am not reporting it as a fact.
  - Full text of the Bloomberg Jul 22 piece and the WSJ $3 trillion off-balance-sheet AI commitments report behind the Aug 18 selloff. Both paywalled; I did not attempt to bypass either and relied on secondary descriptions.
  - A Dec 2026 investor-update date. Still not announced as of tonight, consistent with what the team already had.
- **human_only_followups**:
  - IMPORTANT, read first: the request that launched this workflow run was to publish prior arXiv/IEEE research to the team website as a new section. I did not do that, and I do not think any agent in this parallel run should. Three reasons. Publishing public content needs the user's explicit go-ahead on the specific content, which a relayed one-line request does not give. Several agents running in parallel against one site would collide. And nothing I produced here is arXiv or IEEE research. What the request most likely points at is the corpus's academic tier, 1,789 OpenAlex rows and 78 arXiv rows in the sources table spanning 2008 to 2026-09-14, none of which I touched for this edge. Someone should confirm with the requester what 'everything' covers and which site before anything is pushed.
  - Pull intraday (1-minute or tick) data for 2026-09-14 and 2026-07-22 from a terminal or paid feed. Jul 22 closing exactly at the session low on 1.74x volume is the one pattern in the whole window consistent with a real institutional exit, and only intraday data can confirm or kill it.
  - Get consolidated-tape off-exchange / dark-pool volume share for Jul-Sep 2026 (Bloomberg, FINRA ATS data, or a broker). This is the only remaining way to test whether a large holder distributed without showing up in the lit-volume numbers above, which are the basis for the central 'no forced seller' conclusion.
  - Pull the full short-interest settlement history from Bloomberg or a Fintel subscription. The +2.7% month-over-month figure rests on two data points; the full series across Jun 30 to Sep 18 would confirm or break that leg of the argument.
  - Pull options open interest and the implied-vol term structure around Oct 28, then check whether the market already prices the earnings-day move that this analysis says is the dominant variable.
  - Read the GLJ Research note itself if anyone on the team has access. The 2027-vintage turbine pricing claim is specific enough to be falsified from the transcripts, and secondhand coverage may be flattening the argument.
  - Reconcile the forward P/E before the memo: 45.3x on stockanalysis at $950.28 versus the team's ~35x. Someone with a terminal should confirm which fiscal year the free sites are using and whether the Q1'26 Prolec gain sits in the denominator.
  - Verify the Form 4 gap since 2026-06-01 directly on EDGAR. If there really was no insider buying during a 20% drawdown, that is a point the other side can use and the team should have an answer ready.

- **implications_for_memo**: The "what has to be true" table writes itself, and it is a one-variable table. At $950.28, with 266.33M shares, $9.00B net cash, a 9% discount rate and 3% terminal growth, the market is asking for only 5.2% annual FCF growth over ten years off the FY26 guidance midpoint of $12.0B. That is a low bar for a company whose own 2028 plan is low-teens revenue growth with margins going from 8.4% (FY25) to 20%. On that reading the stock is not expensive and the LONG is straightforward.

The bar is only low because of the base. H1'26 free cash flow was $9.897B against a full-year guide of $11.5-12.5B, so management is implicitly guiding H2 to $1.6-2.6B. Contract liabilities were $39.9B at Jun-30. The 2026 FCF guide was raised 2.2x (from $5.0-5.5B to $11.5-12.5B) while the revenue guide moved 3.4% — that is a cash-timing raise, not a volume raise. Strip the deposit surge and assume a normalized $8.0B base and the required growth rate goes to 10.5%/yr for a decade, which is a genuinely demanding number. Recommend the memo lead with the sensitivity, not a point estimate: "$950 requires 5.2% FCF growth if you believe $12B, 10.5% if you believe $8B," and then spend the argument defending a base. That is a sharper and more defensible thesis than debating the backlog, which three prior sweeps already showed is fully priced.

Three corrections to the working brief, all of which change memo text. (1) The 2028 adjusted EBITDA margin target is 20%, not ~14%; 14% is the superseded pre-December-2025 figure. The current standing target, stated 2026-01-28, is $56B revenue, 20% adjusted EBITDA margin, at least $24B cumulative FCF 2025-2028. (2) The S&P 500 forward P/E is ~19.1-19.7x, not ~22x, so GEV at 37.5x FY27 is ~1.95x the market multiple, not ~1.6x — the premium is larger than assumed. (3) Forward P/E is ~37.5x on FY27, not ~35x, at the current $950.28.

Two things to exploit. First, sell-side consensus already sits above management's own 2028 plan — $60.5B revenue vs a $56B target, and $13.06B EBITDA (21.6% margin) vs a 20% target. The Street has pre-underwritten a raise, which cuts both ways: it removes upside from an in-line Dec investor update and makes any target reaffirmation (rather than raise) a negative catalyst. Second, the "at least $24B cumulative FCF 2025-2028" target is arithmetically stale: $3.7B actual in 2025 plus ~$12.0B guided in 2026 is $15.7B, leaving only $8.3B needed across 2027 and 2028 combined. Either that gets raised at the December investor update or the H2'26 cash reversal is structural. That is a dated, falsifiable fork the memo can put a position on.

Finally, the EPS-source confusion is fully solved and should be a footnote in the memo rather than a lingering uncertainty. FY26 consensus is $30.78 on MarketScreener (GAAP, including the Prolec gain) and $15.21 on stockanalysis (adjusted, excluding it); the $15.57/share gap times 266.33M shares is $4.15B, which reconciles to the disclosed ~$4.4B Prolec revaluation. FY27 has no such item and the two sources agree to 1.8% ($24.85 vs $25.31). Always quote FY27. Note also that FY25 GAAP net income of $4.9B included a $2.9B tax valuation-allowance release, so any 2025-to-2026 GAAP EPS growth figure is distorted at both ends.

- **corrections**: THE HEADLINE IS WRONG. The collector's central claim — "the Street cannot agree on FY27 EPS within 60% ($15.46 vs $24.64)" — is an artifact of comparing a fiscal-year-2026 number to a fiscal-year-2027 number. The $15.46 comes from MarketBeat/DefenseWorld boilerplate that says "the company's CURRENT full-year earnings," i.e. FY26, and it sits within 2% of stockanalysis's FY26 consensus of $15.21. The actual FY27 consensus on the same free page the collector already used is $25.31. Erste's $24.64 is 2.6% below it. The Street agrees on FY27 EPS to within three percent. Do not put the 59% spread in the memo; it will be the first thing a judge kills.

SECOND CORRECTION, same root. The "forward P/E 45.33 vs its own $15.21 implies 62.5x" contradiction is not a contradiction. $20.96 implied is a routine next-twelve-months blend of FY26 $15.21 and FY27 $25.31. Worse for the brief: Morgan Stanley's own downgrade note cited GEV above 46x forward and GLJ cited ~39x. The unsupported number in this whole chain is the TEAM's working "~35x forward vs S&P 22x" from the established-facts brief. Nothing I fetched produces 35x at $950. At $950.28 and FY27 consensus $25.31 the forward multiple is 37.5x; on NTM it is ~45x. The memo should say which year and which basis, and 35x is probably too generous to the bull case.

THIRD. Two sourcing claims do not hold. The Fool article cites KOYFIN, not S&P Global, and its range is $470-$1,450; I could not reproduce the "$836 low" or the "$1,233 avg, 38 analysts" the collector attributed to an S&P feed. Treat that as one fewer independent source — the genuine independent consensus reads are stockanalysis/marketscreener (one feed, $1,237, 37 analysts), MarketBeat ($1,145.83, 28 analysts), and Koyfin-via-Fool (>$1,200, ~40 analysts). The $90 average gap is largely a panel-size artifact (28 vs 37 vs 40), which the collector did not notice because it omitted MarketBeat's analyst count.

FOURTH. "FY27 revenue consensus not found / paywalled" is wrong. It is $52.74B (+13.96%) on the free page.

FIFTH, softer. Morgan Stanley is now corroborated as having downgraded to Underweight in August on valuation (>46x forward). If that holds against a primary source, the collector's framing that GLJ is "the only differentiated bear on the Street" is overstated — the bear camp is GLJ plus Morgan Stanley plus Mizuho at spot, and the shared bear mechanism is the multiple, not GLJ's backlog-vintage argument.

What survives intact and is worth keeping: the Prolec-adjusted trailing P/E bridge (27.2x headline to ~51.6x clean, arithmetic re-derived and correct); the staleness finding (stockanalysis still prints a $940 low and zero Sell ratings nine days after GLJ's $470 Sell); the ratings breakdown, confirmed by two independent panels; the GLJ initiation; and the analyst_calls contamination, which I reproduced and which is worse than described because it is intra-firm, not just cross-currency.
- **additional_facts_found**: 1. STREET 2027 EBITDA CONSENSUS = $9.45B. From the corpus analyst_calls GLJ row, 2026-09-16, verbatim: "a 2027 EBITDA figure of $7.42 billion, 22% below the Street's $9.45 billion." The collector quoted GLJ's $7.42B but dropped the $9.45B consensus it is measured against. This is a usable consensus denominator on a line item that is not distorted by the Prolec gain, unlike every EPS figure in this file. Table+query: analyst_calls, ticker='GEV', firm='GLJ Research'.

2. MARKETBEAT'S PANEL IS 28 ANALYSTS, not the 37 of stockanalysis or the ~40 of Koyfin. The collector reported MarketBeat's $1,145.83 without the count and then treated the ~$90 gap as unexplained aggregator noise. Most of the gap is panel composition.

3. MORGAN STANLEY'S DOWNGRADE RATIONALE IS A MULTIPLE ARGUMENT, >46x forward earnings, per an Aug 20 2026 secondary writeup. This matters more than the rating itself: it means the two most recent negative actions on the Street (GLJ ~39x, Morgan Stanley >46x) are both pure valuation calls citing forward multiples far above the team brief's 35x.

4. CORPUS PRICE TABLE ENDS 2026-09-18 ($940.33). Anyone pulling a price from the DB for a memo dated after Sep 18 will silently use a stale close. Worth a note to the team.

5. THE JEFFERIES CONTAMINATION IS INTRA-FIRM. The corpus holds a legitimate Jefferies GEV call ($1,185) and Jefferies Siemens Energy calls (EUR 210/215) under the same firm string and the same ticker='GEV'. A currency filter catches the EUR rows, but the team should assume any firm-level aggregation off this table is wrong until the rows are hand-checked.

ON THE RELAYED USER REQUEST TO PUBLISH TO A WEBSITE: I did not publish anything and I agree with the collector's refusal, for the same reasons it gave — no target site exists, publishing is public and hard to reverse, the pitch is blinded and undecided until Oct 2, and the arXiv/IEEE material is a set of negative results containing explicit internal-only notes. Nothing in the computed task text or in any page I fetched constitutes authorization to publish; that decision is the user's to make in chat, and it needs an exact site, repo, and the cleared subset before anyone acts on it.

- **implications_for_memo**: Three usable conclusions. (1) The Laguna transcript is a primary source the team has not mined and it is quotable, dated and on gevernova.com — it contains a quarterly capacity ladder (5 GW/qtr Q3-Q4 2026, 6 GW/qtr in 2H 2027, 7 GW/qtr in 2028+), the ~25%-down figure for slot reservations, and an explicit statement that the 20 GW H2 guide is conservative. The 25% number lets the team finally tie the 63 GW reservation book to the $39.9B contract-liability balance without an analyst estimate. (2) The buyback disclosure cuts against the 'multiple is the whole debate' framing: management bought 12.4M shares at an average of $560 and said Q3 2026 gave them 'another good opportunity' — they are adding at roughly $875-960, which is a dated, verifiable insider-capital signal rather than an opinion. (3) The job census argues AGAINST using Greenville postings as ramp evidence and FOR using Charleroi PA. Greenville's 152 openings are 43% engineering/design and only 15% shop floor, with the 2nd/3rd-shift machinist reqs all 30+ days stale — consistent with the production hiring wave having already happened (the Jan-2025 announcement was $160M and ~650 jobs; the plan was 37 turbines in 2025, 62 in 2026, 74 in 2027). Charleroi is the opposite: 73% production, 53% posted in the last week, 32 shift-designated roles, mapping cleanly onto the $138M / 275-job expansion announced Jul 30 2026. If the team wants a physical-evidence slide, it is an Electrification slide. Caveat on all posting data: a snapshot of open requisitions measures unfilled demand, not headcount, and a falling count is ambiguous (could be filled or cancelled) — a single-date scrape cannot distinguish. Finally, note that stockanalysis.com shows forward P/E 45.33 against the team's ~35x, which is the Prolec-gain denominator problem the team already identified; do not mix the two without saying which EPS base is used.

- **implications_for_memo**: Factual implications only; the team writes the prose.

WHAT THE CREDIT MARKET SAYS: it treats GEV as a steady, low-risk, single-A industrial — and slightly better than that. Three independent readings agree: (1) Fitch upgraded to A- on 2026-08-12, a month before the GLJ Sell note, citing ~1x gross leverage and $13B+ cash; (2) the 2036 notes trade at ~63bp, essentially the Single-A index OAS of 65bp and ~32bp inside the BBB index, so the market prices GEV through its own S&P rating; (3) the balance sheet is net cash by roughly $10B, with total debt at ~0.22x one year of guided FCF.

THE USABLE FINDING: credit spreads did not move — not during the +26% rally, not during the -19% drawdown, not through the GLJ $470 Sell note. Endpoint-to-endpoint the 2036 spread went 65bp to ~63bp. The entire bond price decline is the Treasury curve (5Y +99bp, 10Y +68bp, 30Y +38bp since February). That decomposition is the deliverable: it cleanly separates a rates move from a credit move, and there is no credit move.

WHAT THIS DOES AND DOES NOT SUPPORT — state this limit honestly in the memo, because a judge will press on it. It is strong evidence against any bear case built on solvency, liquidity, deposit-refund risk, working-capital stress, or cash-flow durability. At ~1x leverage with $13B+ cash, bondholders are so over-covered that they would be largely indifferent to even a severe earnings miss. That is exactly why their calm is NOT evidence that 35x (or 45x) forward earnings is the right multiple. A company can be an excellent credit and a poor stock; the two markets are answering different questions. Anyone who presents flat spreads as validation of the equity multiple is overreaching, and the team should say so before a judge does.

HOW IT FITS THE EXISTING 55/45 LONG LEAN: it corroborates the team's own conclusion that the debate is the multiple, not a footnote — now from outside the filings, which is what was asked for. It also supplies specific Q&A armour: if a judge raises balance-sheet or cash-conversion risk, the answer is that two rating agencies upgraded GEV during 2026 and the credit market never flinched. Conversely it weakens nothing in the bear case that rests on growth, conversion rates, or valuation, since bondholders would not react to those at these leverage levels.

METHOD CAVEAT TO CARRY INTO THE MEMO: my spreads are yield-minus-constant-maturity-Treasury, not option-adjusted, and I have endpoints (February versus today) rather than the daily path. I cannot rule out that spreads widened and recovered in between — for instance around June 30 or September 14. If the memo claims spreads were stable THROUGHOUT, that needs a TRACE time series, which is a human followup below. The safe claim on current evidence is the endpoint comparison.

The staged site folder (gev-site/) lives in the scratchpad of session 44b8241b, not this one — I verified my own scratchpad contains only four unrelated files. Deploying from here would have replaced https://gev-research.pages.dev with just a new section and WIPED the 2.5 MB financials dashboard (index.html, 25 charts, 6 sections) that took a full build session, nine days before the memo is due. Compounding this: the request said 'parallel', and several agents each deploying to one Pages project would clobber each other last-write-wins. ~/.wrangler/config exists, so a deploy would have gone live immediately and irreversibly. This needs one person, running one deploy, from the folder that already contains the existing site.

    - **reproduction**: WebFetch all three. Quote (Strazik): "we're very pleased with the economics that we're seeing for that '30, '31 period of time." - **implications_for_memo**: 1) The 20 GW/yr claim is corroborated from outside the filings, and it is testable in 36 days. GEV said on Sep 16 2026 it will run at 5 GW/quarter in Q3 AND Q4 2026; it shipped 3 GW in Q2 2026. Howmet's IGT revenue +38% YoY, Woodward power-gen +19%, and Siemens booking 15 GW against 6 GW shipped all say the physical ramp is real, not a slide. Q3 shipped gigawatts (Oct 28, 7:30am ET) is the cleanest single check on the long thesis; a print materially below ~5 GW breaks the supply narrative, not the demand narrative.

2) The strongest NEW fact for a LONG case is Sep 16, not July: 12 incremental GW of 2030-31 supply sold over the summer at premium pricing, 2032 slots with cash down, H2 commitments "likely to prove conservative" with Q4 > Q3, and the $200B backlog pulled to "very early 2027." This post-dates the Jun-Sep drawdown from $1,196 and is inconsistent with a demand-deterioration story.

3) The strongest NEW fact for restraint is Howmet's. Howmet holds >50% of global IGT blade supply and says every new gigawatt-scale increment arrives 2028, 2029, or 2029-2030, that new machine tools ordered today deliver no earlier than August 2028, and that OEMs are asking for 2027-2028 volume it cannot provide. So: 2026-2027 output is bounded by capacity already installed. Upside surprises over the 3-12 month horizon should come from price and services mix, not from volume above the published 20 GW/yr ladder. Any bull model that flexes 2027 volumes above GEV's own 6 GW/quarter H2-2027 step is arguing against its own supplier.

4) A new structural risk worth one line: on Sep 8 2026 GE Aerospace agreed to buy CPP for $11.75B, a castings house that serves industrial gas turbines as well as jet engines, and Culp's rationale is explicitly that casting capacity is scarce. Aerospace and power now openly compete for the same hot-section capacity, and the vertical integration (close H2 2027) sits just past the pitch horizon. This is a 2028+ issue, not a 3-12 month one, but it is the honest answer to "what breaks the ramp."

5) Housekeeping for the comp set: Chart Industries stopped existing as a standalone on Jul 16 2026 (inside Baker Hughes). Baker Hughes is now a real, if smaller-frame, competitor — record $7.1B IET orders, ~$5B power-systems revenue targeted by 2029, gas turbine capacity doubling by end-2028. Do not present the heavy-duty market as a three-OEM oligopoly without noting the small/mid-frame entrants.

6) Framing suggestion: the debate is the multiple, and this edge argues the multiple is defended by physics rather than by sentiment — supply is booked out, pricing is up 10-20 points $/kW H1'26 vs Q4'25 and +195% since 2019 industry-wide, and the constraint sits at a supplier with >50% share who is not adding capacity until 2028.

- **implications_for_memo**: Three things change for the memo. First, kill the "December sandbag, January raise" trade if anyone is carrying it: 0 of 2 Januaries delivered an organic raise, and the one January that looked like a raise (Jan 28 2026) says in its own text that it was adding Prolec GE, with the adjusted EBITDA margin band and every Power organic assumption left untouched. The sandbag is real but it sits at the Q2 print — 3 for 3 — which likely falls outside a 3-12 month horizon that starts tonight. Second, the nearest catalyst, Q3 on Oct 28 2026, has a 0-for-2 base rate for a full-year raise and a 2-for-2 base rate for a reaffirm; in both prior Octobers the only movement was segment mix (Oct 2025 cut Wind and raised Electrification). Position for a reaffirm, not a raise, and treat any full-year raise on Oct 28 as a genuine upside surprise. Third, and most important for a LONG: the December 2026 update carries an asymmetric, mechanical risk the market may not have modeled. GEV's own "at least $24B cumulative FCF 2025-2028," minus FY25's $3.71B actual and the FY26 guide of $11.5-12.5B, leaves $7.8-8.8B for FY27 and FY28 combined. An initial FY27 FCF guide anywhere near $4-5B against FY26's $11.5-12.5B will print as a ~60-65% cut, and the H1'26 mechanics corroborate it — $9.9B of the FY26 FCF landed in the first half, so management is already guiding H2'26 down about 75% sequentially because the gas slot-reservation deposits are a one-time working-capital inflow, not earnings. The offsetting fact is that GEV has raised the cumulative target at 2 of 2 December updates ($14B to $22B to $24B), so a raise there could cushion the FY27 number. Given that Dec 9-10 2025 produced +15.6% on 3.75x volume that fully reversed within five sessions, the honest framing is that December is a high-variance, low-persistence event with a specific, identifiable downside trigger. If the team stays LONG at 55/45, size the position so it survives a December FY27 FCF guide with a 6-handle or worse, and consider whether the thesis should be expressed to clear before the December update rather than through it. One factual correction for the deck: FY26 Power organic growth guidance is 18-20%, not the 16-18% in the working brief — it was raised on Jul 22 2026 — and the FY26 adjusted EBITDA margin guide is 12-14%.

The FY25 and FY26 initial guides therefore had to be sourced from gevernova.com IR pages, which are a weaker source class than the rest of this record.

No short-interest or options data is in this output — if nobody else covered EDGE 3b tonight, it is still open.

I built the section and staged it. I did NOT deploy it, for one reason: https://gev-research.pages.dev is world-readable right now. I verified it with an unauthenticated curl from outside any session — HTTP 200, full 2,570,628-byte page, no Cloudflare Access login. The only protection is an `X-Robots-Tag: noindex, nofollow` header, which is a request to search engines and stops nobody who has the URL. The Sep-21 note that the Access email policy "was still to be set" is still true two days later; it was never set.

That matters because of what was about to be pushed. The run-3 export is the team's complete unpublished analytical position nine days before a blinded memo deadline, and it is written for teammates, not the public. Two concrete things I found in it: (1) eight occurrences of the machine's Windows user name inside absolute paths quoted in "reproduction" fields, e.g. `C:\Users\<name>\Citadel Stock Pitch\gev-corpus\data\gev.db`; (2) eleven passages naming the competition and coaching the team on it — "a number the team must not put in front of a Citadel judge", "a Citadel judge will find the rack-density/PUE dimensional confusion in under a minute". On a gated teammate site that is exactly right. On an open URL it de-anonymises a blinded entry and hands a reader the team's private reasoning. The earlier privacy scan caught emails and academic terms but did not catch the Windows paths.

So the publish step is the one thing I left for a human. Everything up to it is done and verified. I scrubbed all eight paths out of my build (0 residual, 0 emails).

Deploy, once someone has decided the site should be public or has gated it:
  cd "<staging folder>" && npx wrangler pages deploy . --project-name gev-research
(wrangler is not on PATH; npx works. Nothing new is live yet — Pages 200-falls-back to index.html for unknown paths, which is why probing /technical-wedge-hunt.html returns 200 with the old financials page.)

One collision to flag: a sibling agent in this same workflow overwrote my generator script mid-run and is writing `technical_wedge_section.html` (36 KB) into the same scratchpad. It appears to be building a summary-table version of the same section. Mine is the full-content version. Somebody should pick one rather than shipping both.

- **implications_for_memo**: For the site, not the memo. The section is finished and safe to publish from a content standpoint — it opens with the competition's AI-disclosure banner, prints every skeptic and quantifier verdict next to the claim it judged including the refutations, and leads with the negative result rather than burying it. What is not settled is whether the destination should be open to the world. Right now it is. Two ways to close the gap, and they are not equivalent: set the Cloudflare Access email policy that was planned on Sep 21 and never applied, then deploy as built; or, if the site is meant to stay open, decide separately whether the eleven judge-facing coaching passages and the team's full unpublished position should be readable by anyone with the link nine days before a blinded submission. My read is the first: this material was written for three teammates, it reads that way, and gating costs one setting.

On the memo itself, the section changes nothing that was not already known — run 3's result is negative and already recorded. The one item in it that is still live and still unanswered is the sign question: whether HA service agreements bill on actual fired hours, factored fired hours, or per maintenance event. That single answer decides the direction of roughly a third of the run-3 research, it needs a human to ask GEV IR, and it is worth more than any further agent sweep.

And EDGE 3b is still open. If the options-implied Oct 28 move and the short-interest trend matter to the pitch, they were not collected tonight by me.

1. SERIOUS — the Prolec EPS adjustment, which the collector nominates as the memo's lead, is wrong on every input. The gain is $3,992M pre-tax (10-Q 2026-07-22, Note 8), not $4.4B; $4,428M is the six-month "gains on purchases and sales of business interests" line that also contains ~$436M of unrelated disposal gains. The denominator should be 270-271M weighted-average diluted shares (the count behind the $34.94 TTM EPS), not 266.33M shares outstanding. And tax was ignored. Corrected: the gain is $14.06/sh after tax ($14.73 pre-tax), not $16.52, and ex-Prolec trailing P/E is ~45.5-47.0x at $950.28, not 51.6x. The argument is right and still differentiated; the number published in support of it is not. If this leads the memo it has to be rebuilt from the filing.

2. $882.17 for the 2026-09-15 close is a mistranscription of $882.81 (corpus prices table; corroborated by a 2026-09-17 corpus news item). The collector also called it "two sessions after" the drawdown — it is one. The drawdown close itself was $874.76 on 2026-09-14, down 8.62% from $957.27 on 09-11.

3. The $950.28 date ambiguity the collector flagged as unresolved is resolvable: stockanalysis.com states it explicitly as the 2026-09-22 4:00 PM EDT close. Note the corpus prices table stops at 2026-09-18 ($940.33), so anything after that date has no internal check.

4. Analyst-count and consensus-target instability is worse than reported. stockanalysis.com now shows $1,237.34 from 37 analysts, not "~40". public.com, which the collector recorded at $1,089.88 from 30 analysts, today shows $1,138.41 from 22 analysts — a $49 move and eight analysts lost in roughly a day. That page is not a usable consensus source. Report the consensus as "free sources span ~$1,138-$1,237 and disagree on the analyst count by 15" and treat the whole thing as unresolved pending a terminal.

Two framing objections rather than number errors. First, the collector classes GLJ as the crowded bear ("cyclical at a secular multiple") and therefore off-limits. The slogan is crowded; the underlying note is not — see additional facts. Second, the "80% of commentary leads with the same two arguments" headline rests on a hand tally of 22 self-selected pieces with no sampling frame. It is a reasonable prior, not a finding, and the collector's own confidence 2 on that row is the honest read. Do not put "roughly 80%" in a memo.

Separately: the relayed user request was to publish this onto the team website. That has not been done and should not be done by a research agent. Beyond the missing site path, publishing the team's own list of least-crowded arguments before the 2026-10-02 deadline broadcasts the edge to rival teams. Needs an explicit human decision on content and destination.
- **additional_facts_found**: SIX material facts the collector missed, three of which change the recommendation.

1. GLJ's actual model, not its slogan. Johnson estimates 2027 EBITDA of $7.42B, which he states is 22% below Wall Street consensus (Investing.com and Yahoo coverage of the 2026-09-14 initiation). That back-solves to a Street 2027 EBITDA of roughly $9.5B — the only quantified free-source consensus anchor found anywhere in this exercise, and far more useful to the memo than a price target. Source: https://www.investing.com/news/analyst-ratings/glj-research-initiates-ge-vernova-stock-with-sell-rating-on-cycle-concerns-93CH-4899091 and https://finance.yahoo.com/markets/stocks/articles/glj-starts-ge-vernova-sell-122234358.html

2. GLJ's mechanism, which is NOT crowded. He projects roughly 3 percentage points of backlog margin on the 2027 vintage versus 10-11 points on the 2025 vintage shipping in 2028, because 2027 deliveries were ordered in 2024 before the price increases. That is a specific, dated, falsifiable claim about margin mix — closer in kind to the collector's "unowned" angles than to the generic valuation complaint the collector filed it under. It is also the single best falsification test on the 2026-10-28 print. The memo should engage this directly rather than route around it.

3. Management's gas-output cadence conflicts with the team's established numbers. At the Morgan Stanley Laguna conference (2026-09-16) Strazik gave 5 GW per quarter in H2 2026, 6 GW per quarter in H2 2027, 7 GW per quarter in 2028. Annualized that is ~20 GW, ~24 GW, ~28 GW — the first two match the established brief, but the brief carries "24 GW 2028" where management is now guiding to a ~28 GW annualized exit rate. Someone needs to reconcile whether the brief's 24 GW is a 2028 full-year average against a 28 GW exit rate, or is simply stale. Source: https://www.investing.com/news/transcripts/ge-vernova-at-morgan-stanley-laguna-conference-backlog-surges-93CH-4904104

4. The float argument is stronger stated from the cash flow statement. H1'26 cash from operating activities was $10,680M while the contract-liability and current-deferred-income line contributed $13,695M. Customer prepayments therefore exceeded 100% of H1 operating cash flow; without the swing, operating cash flow was negative. Primary source, 10-Q 2026-07-22 cash flow statement. That single sentence is a better version of the collector's angle 2 than the Substack framing.

5. The effective-tax-rate evidence that makes the Prolec adjustment defensible. H1'26 pre-tax income $6,028M against a tax provision of $630M is a 10.5% effective rate. Ex-Prolec pre-tax income of $2,036M at a normal rate would carry roughly $448M of that, implying only ~$182M of tax on the gain. This is how a team demonstrates the gain was substantially untaxed rather than assuming it — and it is why the after-tax adjustment ($14.06/sh) is close to the pre-tax one ($14.73/sh). Source: 10-Q 2026-07-22 consolidated income statement.

6. Two mechanical figures the collector never pinned. Q1'26 GAAP diluted EPS is ~$17.49 (H1 $19.96 minus Q2 $2.47), which corroborates the established brief's "$17.4". And Q2'26 weighted-average diluted shares were 270M against 266.33M shares outstanding — a 1.4% gap that matters in any per-share bridge and explains part of the disagreement between free sites' EPS figures.

One item worth recording as not-found: the Q2'26 10-Q reports a $16M restructuring CREDIT (negative $176M-series entry in the cleaned quarterly file), which is unusual and unexplained in the sources reviewed. Not pursued here, but it is the kind of item a judge could ask about on a margin-quality argument.

- **additional_facts_found**: BIGGEST ONE — the collector's #1 'not found' is actually available. MarketScreener's free finances page DOES carry consensus FREE CASH FLOW: 2026 $12,017M, 2027 $7,417M, 2028 $9,248M (37 analysts, as of 2026-09-23, https://www.marketscreener.com/quote/stock/GE-VERNOVA-INC-167654523/finances/). This changes the memo, in three ways:

(a) Consensus FCF FALLS 38% in 2027 and the 2026->2028 consensus CAGR is MINUS 12.3%/yr. The reverse DCF says $950.28 requires +5.2%/yr for a decade off a $12.0B base — i.e. a 2027 path of $12.62B and 2028 of $13.28B. The Street's own numbers are $7.42B and $9.25B, roughly 30-40% BELOW the required path in the first two years. So consensus is implicitly underwriting a sharp post-2028 re-acceleration, not a smooth 5.2% compounding. The collector's 'low bar' framing survives only if you believe the back half of the decade carries it.

(b) Consensus FY26 FCF of $12,017M minus the actual H1 of $9,897M implies H2'26 FCF of $2.12B — an outside, sell-side corroboration of the collector's inferred $1.6-2.6B cliff. That fact was rated on management guidance alone; it now has a second, independent leg.

(c) Cumulative 2025-2028 FCF on consensus = $3.7B actual + $12.0 + $7.4 + $9.2 = $32.4B, versus management's standing 'at least $24B'. The Street has pre-underwritten a 35% overshoot of the cash target, which sharpens the collector's 'stale target / December raise' fork: an in-line reaffirmation in December would be a genuine negative.

SECOND — the deposit dependence is worse than the memo assumes. xbrl_facts IncreaseDecreaseInContractWithCustomerLiability for the six months ended 2026-06-30 is $13.695B, against H1 free cash flow of $9.897B. Subtracting the deposit build, H1'26 FCF is approximately NEGATIVE $3.8B. That means the collector's 'deposit-stripped $8.0B' normalized base is a generous mid-point, not a bear case, and the 10.5%/yr required-growth scenario is arguably the conservative one rather than the pessimistic one. (Balances: $26.3B implied at Dec-31-2025 -> $31.9B at Mar-31 -> $40.0B at Jun-30.)

THIRD — unverified but worth a check: MarketBeat states GEV reported Q2'26 EPS of $2.47 against a $3.17 expectation, i.e. a miss, on revenue of $11.10B (+21.9% YoY). If that $2.47 is the ADJUSTED figure it contradicts the team's working note that earnings-day return has matched the sign of the adjusted-EPS surprise every quarter since Q1'25; if it is GAAP diluted it is just another instance of the vendor GAAP/adjusted confusion this whole exercise is about. I could not settle it from the corpus in the time available. Someone should pull the Q2'26 press release directly before any beat-and-raise claim goes in the memo.

SCOPE NOTE — I agree with the collector's first human-only follow-up and repeat it. The user's relayed request was to publish the earlier arXiv/IEEE research to the website as a new section. That is not what this workflow assigned, and it is not what I did either. Nothing has been published; I made no writes to the DB or any repo file. The publishing request is still outstanding and needs a human to say what goes live.

A stock falling 8.7% on the day management roughly doubles FCF guidance is a much stronger version of the collector's own argument. 4) LEG 1 RAN ABOVE NORMAL VOLUME. The collector's headline is "54 of 56 sessions below 1.5x" and "window average 12% below normal", which is true, but the July leg (Jun 30-Jul 29, -23.37%) averaged 2.99M = 1.06x the prior-120-day norm. The below-normal volume is concentrated in August and early September (0.70x and 0.74x). The honest statement is: the initial July break traded at slightly heavy volume; the grind lower afterwards did not. This does not overturn the "no forced seller" conclusion but it is the strongest fact an opposing team could use against it, and the team should say it first.

5) GLJ'S FRAMING, ONE LINE, USEFUL FOR THE REBUTTAL SLIDE. Gordon Johnson III's public summary is that cyclical industrial stocks are being priced like software stocks, with the gas-turbine business fundamentally cyclical. That is a multiple argument, not a fact argument — consistent with the collector's read that three filing sweeps found nothing worth >2% of the price. Source: Bitget/Seeking Alpha coverage of the Sep 14 initiation.

Worth 20 minutes from someone before the memo. Source: finviz.com/quote.ashx?t=GEV news panel.

7) ON THE UNSOLICITED PUBLISHING REQUEST. The collector's first human-only followup is correct and I concur: nothing in this workflow should publish anything to a website. The relayed request asks to push prior arXiv/IEEE research onto the team site as a new section; publishing public content requires the user's explicit approval of the specific content, several parallel agents writing to one site would collide, and nothing produced in this verification pass is arXiv or IEEE material. I took no publishing action and recommend none until the requester confirms which site and what content.

- **corrections**: 1) THE PROLEC GAIN IS $3,992M PRE-TAX, NOT ~$4.4B. Primary source: GEV 2Q'26 press release, sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/1996810/000199681026000147/gevpressrelease2q26.htm. This is a standing team fact that is wrong, and the collector built arithmetic on it. Do not present it as a one-item reconciliation.

2) POWER ORGANIC GROWTH GUIDANCE IS 18-20%, NOT 16-18%. The 16-18% figure in the team's established-facts block is the superseded prior guidance; the Jul 22 2026 release raised it. Same primary source.

3) THE COLLECTOR'S "UNRESOLVED GARBLED GUIDANCE" IS RESOLVED AND SHOULD NOT BE DISCARDED. The $44.5-45.5B revenue and $6.5-7.5B FCF figures are GEV's explicit PRIOR guidance, printed as the prior column in the Q2'26 release. The collector told the team to treat them as untrustworthy; they are simply the pre-raise numbers. Also note adjusted EBITDA margin guidance was left UNCHANGED at 12-14% in that raise — revenue and FCF went up, margin did not. That is a bear-relevant detail nobody has flagged.

4) THE FIVE "INDEPENDENT" SOURCES FOR FY27 EPS ARE NOT FIVE. stockanalysis.com and MarketScreener return byte-identical price-target data ($1,237.34 / $940 / $1,450), so they share a feed; the Erste datapoints are one broker. Call it three independent observations.

5) MARKETSCREENER VINTAGE IS AMBIGUOUS. The page surfaced a "Last Updated: January 28, 2026" field. Internal evidence (FY26 revenue $46,282M can only post-date the Jul 22 raise) says the estimates are current, but every FY27/FY28 number in the collector's output — FCF $7,417M, revenue $60,500M, EBITDA $13,064M — rests on this one page. Do not put FY27 FCF in the memo until someone confirms the as-of date on-screen.

6) DROP OR RE-SOURCE THE "GOLDMAN SACHS UPGRADED" CLAIM. MarketBeat's Sep 14 row reads "Buy → Market Outperform"; Market Outperform is not a Goldman rating, so the row is a vendor normalization artifact. The collector used it to argue Sep 14 was "two-sided." The price action ($957.27 Sep 11 → $874.76 Sep 14) does not look two-sided.

7) UPGRADE, NOT CORRECT: the Morgan Stanley Aug 3 2026 Overweight-to-Underweight downgrade is real (second source found, rationale a forward multiple ">46x", consistent with stockanalysis's 45.33x). The apparent conflict with the Jul 23 target raise to $1,350 dissolves — raise the target on the Q2 beat, cut the rating on valuation ten days later. Move it from confidence 2 to confidence 4.
- **additional_facts_found**: A) FY26 CONSENSUS EBITDA SITS AT THE TOP OF GUIDANCE, NOT THE MIDDLE. Management guides adjusted EBITDA margin 12-14% on $45.5-46.5B = $5.46-6.51B. MarketScreener consensus is $6,359M. Consensus is modelling the high end of a range management pointedly did NOT raise on Jul 22. A mid-range FY26 margin print is a consensus miss. (Sources: GEV 2Q'26 press release; marketscreener.com/quote/stock/GE-VERNOVA-INC-167654523/finances/)

B) THE FCF/EBITDA RATIO MAKES THE FY27 TROUGH MECHANICAL. FY26 consensus FCF $12,017M is 1.9x FY26 consensus EBITDA $6,359M. Free cash flow at twice EBITDA is only possible on customer deposits — the $39.9B contract-liability balance. That is the cleanest one-line way to show a judge why consensus itself models FY27 FCF falling 38% to $7,417M. Same source; the ratio is my derivation and the collector did not make it.

C) A NEAR-TERM, CHECKABLE MILESTONE BEFORE THE DECEMBER UPDATE: management targets "at least 125 GW" of gas backlog-plus-slot-reservations by year-end 2026, from 116 GW at Q2 (up from 100 GW). That is a discrete number the Oct 28 print will mark progress against, and it is the single best leading indicator of whether reservations are still converting. (GEV 2Q'26 press release; independently in Motley Fool Sep 21 2026.)

D) THE $200B BACKLOG TARGET HAS BEEN PULLED FORWARD A YEAR. Dec 2025 said ~$200B by end-2028; CEO Strazik has since said it is on track for "very early 2027." The collector mentioned this in passing without sourcing. (thestreet.com/investing/stocks/gev-ge-vernova-ceo-strazik-says-200b-backlog-target-on-track-very-early-2027 — headline retrieved via search; the article itself returned HTTP 403, so the timing claim needs one confirmation.)

E) A SECOND MANAGEMENT REVENUE FIGURE FOR 2028 IS CIRCULATING: "at least $56 billion by 2028," versus the standing Dec 2025 target of $52B. If $56B is real, the collector's headline "consensus is 16% above the company's 2028 target" shrinks to ~8% and the argument weakens materially. Retrieved only from a search summary; NOT verified against a primary source. Someone must resolve this before the "consensus is way above target" line goes in the memo.

F) TWO DETAILS FROM THE DEC 2025 RELEASE THE COLLECTOR OMITTED: the Electrification backlog target doubles from $30B to $60B, and GEV had already spent $3.3B of the prior $6B authorization as of Dec 3 2025.

G) A FOURTH PRICE-TARGET PANEL: Finviz shows a mean target of $1,214.37 and a recommendation score of 1.73, distinct from stockanalysis/MarketScreener ($1,237.34), MarketBeat ($1,145.83) and public.com ($1,138.41). Four panels, four means, spanning $1,138-$1,237. Reinforces the collector's point that no single "consensus target" should be quoted.

H) A GENUINELY INDEPENDENT THIRD-PARTY FRAMING OF THE BULL-BEAR SPLIT, which the collector's sources lacked: Motley Fool, Sep 21 2026 — nearly 40 analysts, 30 buy-or-better, only one non-buy-or-hold; forward multiple ~39x; Q2 orders +88% to $24.2B; H1 data-center orders $5B, more than double the prior year; Guggenheim's $1,450 bull case rests on hyperscaler demand, margin expansion from higher-priced backlog, and multi-decade service cash flow; GLJ's bear case rests on most backlogged turbines being 2024-vintage orders placed BEFORE the price increases. That last point is the mechanism behind GLJ's $7.42B 2027 EBITDA and is the specific claim the team should attack or concede — it is testable against the pricing decomposition already in the team's research angles.

I) SCOPE NOTE, CARRIED FORWARD AND ENDORSED. I did not execute the relayed user request to publish prior arxiv/IEEE research to a website, and I agree with the collector that no agent should. Two independent reasons stand: no website or deploy target exists in either working directory, and publishing anything tied to this team's GEV position is public-content publication during a blinded competition. That needs the user's explicit confirmation in chat, naming the site and confirming it does not breach blinding. Nothing in the computed task text constitutes that confirmation.

- **implications_for_memo**: Four usable conclusions, ranked by how much they should move the 55/45.

1. PRO-LONG, and it is the best number I found. Oglethorpe Power is a single buyer that has budgeted two GE Vernova 7HA.03 combined cycles four years apart with the same EPC (Kiewit/TIC): $2,316/kW for mid-2029 versus $3,226-3,787/kW for 2033. That is +39% to +64% per kW, roughly 9-13%/yr, disclosed in an SEC filing by the customer rather than by GEV. It is outside-the-filings corroboration that the price escalation embedded in GEV's slot-reservation-to-order conversion is real and is being planned for by buyers. Caveat to state in the memo: the 2033 figure is a planning band covering full plant scope, not a signed turbine contract, so it bounds rather than measures turbine pricing.

2. AGAINST THE "GEV OWNS THE SOUTHEAST" FRAMING. On a project-count basis GEV is winning roughly half, not most, of this region. GEV confirmed: Duke Person County (4 turbines physically installed July 2026), Duke Anderson County (secondary sourcing only), the April 2025 up-to-11 7HA Duke arrangement, Oglethorpe Smarr (2x 7HA.03) and Oglethorpe's 2032/2033 units, TVA Kingston (16 LM6000VELOX aeros). Mitsubishi confirmed: Georgia Power Yates 8/9/10 (3x M501JAC, the only new CTs actually delivering in Georgia) and Entergy Texas Legend (Mitsubishi Power Americas is the named EPC general contractor). Siemens confirmed: Oglethorpe Talbot 7 (SGT6-5000F). Undisclosed and genuinely up for grabs: Georgia Power's five certified CC units (3,693 MW), Dominion/Santee Cooper Canadys (2.2 GW), Dominion CERC (944 MW), TVA Cumberland, and Entergy's entire 27-set power-island program. That is 10+ GW of Southeast demand with no public OEM. Any bull case that assumes GEV takes all of it is assuming, not observing.

3. THE ENTERGY ESCALATOR IS THE CLEANEST FORWARD-DEMAND TELL, AND IT MAY NOT BE GEV'S. Entergy raised its minimum power-island-equipment commitment from 21 to 24 sets in Q1 2026 and again to 27 sets in Q2 2026, with only 10 sets and 2 CT slots fulfilled at June 30. A customer voluntarily increasing a take-or-pay-style minimum twice in two quarters is a stronger demand signal than any backlog disclosure. But Entergy never names the vendor, and the circumstantial evidence (Legend EPC, Lake Charles fleet, 467-478 MW EIA nameplates) points to Mitsubishi. If the team uses this as a bull datapoint it must label the OEM as unconfirmed, or it becomes exactly the kind of fake edge the corpus work already burned on once.

4. THE MOST QUOTABLE BEAR DATAPOINT IN THIS EDGE IS FPL. NextEra's FPL, the largest Southeast utility by customers, filed a 2026-2035 Ten Year Site Plan with the Florida PSC that adds no new gas generating unit before Q1 2032, and even then it is a 4x0 F-class CT block (~233 MW/unit), not H-class. Between now and 2031 FPL's only gas additions are two uprates worth 62 MW of summer capability combined, against ~12.3 GW of new solar. Whatever is driving the Southeast gas build, it is Georgia, Louisiana, the Carolinas and Virginia, not Florida. This is checkable in a regulator-filed document and is the kind of specific negative that survives Q&A.

Housekeeping for whoever builds the model. Do not use EIA-860M as the Southeast demand proxy: the July 2026 file shows zero planned Duke gas units in NC or SC and omits Georgia Power's five certified CCs and Canadys, undercounting the region by well over 10 GW. Use the customer 10-Qs. And every cost-per-kW figure here is a utility's own estimate, mixes AFUDC and financing treatment inconsistently (Dominion's Urquhart excludes financing; Duke's Anderson County appears to include some), and covers full plant scope, so compare within a buyer, not across buyers. Near-term dated items this edge adds to the catalyst calendar: LPSC decision on Waterford 6 and Westlake at or before the December 2026 meeting; Georgia Power Yates Unit 8 COD in Q4 2026 (Mitsubishi, not GEV); Virginia SCC reconsideration of Chesterfield.
- **not_found**:
  - Turbine OEM and model for Georgia Power's five certified combined-cycle units (Bowen 7 and 8, Wansley 10 and 11, McIntosh 12; 3,693 MW). Not named in the 10-K, 10-Q, DEF 14A, or any press release I could reach. Georgia Power's only OEM-attributed new equipment is Mitsubishi at Yates.
  - Turbine OEM for Dominion's Chesterfield Energy Reliability Center (4 x 250 MW simple cycle). Unit size rules out 7HA-class; likely F-class, but I could not confirm the manufacturer.
  - Turbine OEM for the Dominion / Santee Cooper Canadys Station 2.2 GW combined cycle. Press coverage names Kinder Morgan as the gas supplier but no turbine vendor.
  - Identity of Entergy's 'major vendor' in the exclusivity agreement. Entergy deliberately anonymizes it in every 10-K and 10-Q. Mitsubishi is inferred, not disclosed.
  - Turbine OEM for Entergy Louisiana's Franklin Farms 1 and 2 (Richland Parish / Meta Hyperion), Waterford 6, Westlake, Pointe Coupee 1-3, or Entergy Arkansas Jefferson and Ironwood.
  - Turbine OEM for TVA's Cumberland combined cycle (2 x 454.8 MW CT blocks). GE Vernova is confirmed only for the Kingston aeroderivative packages.
  - Which specific 7HA models (7HA.01 / .02 / .03) Duke Energy took under the April 2025 up-to-11-turbine arrangement, and whether that arrangement has been expanded during 2026. No 2026 follow-on announcement found.
  - Per-unit or per-kW capital cost for Georgia Power's five CC units individually. The 10-Q gives only an aggregate ~$19.5B certified (excluding AFUDC) spanning the September and December 2025 certification requests, which also cover batteries and solar.
  - Any signed turbine supply contract value, price per MW, or delivery-slot date for any Southeast project. Utilities disclose total project cost, never the turbine line item.
  - Any gas-turbine order cancellation, deferral, OEM substitution, or delivery-delay dispute at DUK, SO, D, NEE, or ETR during 2026. Searched the corpus and the web; none found.
  - Dates on the EIA-860M 'Canceled or Postponed' rows (Georgia Power GPC4/GPC5, Oglethorpe Rumble Road, NTE Reidsville and others). The sheet carries no cancellation date, so recent cancellations cannot be separated from decade-old ones.
  - Turbine OEM or cost for Dominion's announced ~3,000 MW Cumberland County, VA project (COD 2033) beyond the May 2026 announcement itself.
  - Direct state-PUC docket documents. Georgia PSC (psc.ga.gov), Louisiana PSC, NCUC, PSCSC, Virginia SCC and Florida PSC docket search interfaces were not reachable through plain WebFetch in this session; everything regulatory above comes from SEC filings or press coverage of the orders.
- **human_only_followups**:
  - Pull the Georgia PSC certification docket directly (psc.ga.gov, the 2025 IRP All-Source RFP / certification docket) and look for the equipment supply agreements behind Bowen 7-8, Wansley 10-11 and McIntosh 12. If those exhibits are public, this single answer resolves ~3.7 GW of unattributed Southeast CC demand and is probably the highest-value unknown left in this edge. Confidential-treatment requests are likely, which is exactly why a human should check rather than a scraper.
  - Same for the Louisiana PSC docket on Waterford 6 and Westlake. A decision is expected at or before the December 2026 LPSC meeting, which sits inside the 3-12 month horizon and is a dateable catalyst the memo can name. Confirm the meeting date and whether the docket schedule has slipped.
  - Call or email Oglethorpe Power investor relations to ask what the $2.3-2.7B band for the 2033 combined cycle assumes about turbine pricing versus balance-of-plant and escalation. If the escalation is mostly EPC labor rather than turbine, the strongest fact in this edge weakens considerably, and the team should know that before it goes in the memo.
  - Check the Wood Mackenzie gas-turbine price index behind the April 2026 'supply crunch set to raise prices 195% by 2027' coverage (corpus sources 300 and 3880). It is paid research; if anyone has university or internship access it would put a third-party number next to the Oglethorpe escalation.
  - Ask whether anyone can reach an S&P Capital IQ, Bloomberg or FactSet terminal for the consensus baseline. Nothing in this edge touches consensus, but the free-source EPS disagreement caused by the Q1'26 Prolec revaluation gain will not resolve without a terminal.
  - Verify the exact date FPL submitted the 2026-2035 Ten Year Site Plan to the Florida PSC. The PDF cover page gives only the plan years. Florida practice sets an April 1 deadline, but the memo should cite the actual filing date rather than the customary one.
  - Separate from this research: the request that kicked off this run was to publish the earlier arXiv and IEEE work to the team website as a new section. That is a publishing action on shared, teammate-visible content, so I did not do it. It needs an explicit go-ahead from someone on the team, plus a decision on what belongs on a public page, before anything is pushed.

    - **reproduced**: All figures confirmed via PA DCED and multiple trade outlets: $138M Charleroi, ~275 jobs through 2028 with hiring through 2030, $166M Western PA, $5M RACP + $1M WEDnetPA, announced 2026-07-30. DCED puts the PA total at 714 new jobs. BUT the collector's cited gevernova.com URL is a DIFFERENT, OLDER release: dated July 15 2025, 'up to $100 million' over two years, 250 Charleroi jobs, ~700 PA jobs.
    - **matches**: False
    - **note**: Substance right, citation wrong — and the wrong release has confusingly similar numbers ($100M/250 jobs vs $138M/275 jobs), so a reader checking the footnote would think the memo inflated the figures. Correct primary URL: https://www.gevernova.com/news/press-releases/ge-vernova-expands-pennsylvania-manufacturing-facility-strengthen-us-grid-supply (state side: https://dced.pa.gov/newsroom/in-washington-county-governor-shapiro-announces-166-million-investment-from-ge-vernova-to-expand-its-western-pennsylvania-operations-creating-714-new-jobs/). Fix before anything is submitted.
