    - **original**: Announced 2026-09-16; GEV withdrew notice of termination, all claims dismissed; terms confidential; 'GEV had previously claimed it was owed more than $300M'
    - **reproduced**: Settlement, date, withdrawal of termination notice, dismissal of claims and confidentiality of terms all confirmed (gevernova.com release; Boston Globe, New Bedford Light, regional NPR stations Sep 16-17). The claim sizes are BOTH ways: Vineyard Wind said it was owed more than $800 MILLION from GE Vernova over 2024 blade-failure delays, while GEV said Vineyard Wind owed IT more than $300M.
    - **matches**: False
    - **note**: Materially incomplete, in the direction that matters. The collector reported only GEV's $300M receivable claim and omitted the $800M claim AGAINST GEV — i.e. it dropped the entire tail risk the settlement removed. The correct read is that GEV settled away an $800M gross exposure at undisclosed cost, which is a bigger and cleaner de-risking point. Still unknowable: whether cash moved, and GEV's ongoing O&M role.
  - - **fact**: Catalyst calendar from the IR events page
    - **original**: Sep 16 Laguna; Oct 28 2026 Q3 earnings 7:30-8:30am EDT confirmed; no other Sept 2026 conference; no Dec 2026 investor update announced
    - **reproduced**: Fresh WebFetch of https://www.gevernova.com/investors/events confirms Oct 28 2026, 7:30-8:30am EDT, Q3 2026 earnings webcast, and the Sep 16 Laguna fireside. No December 2026 event listed. Page also lists a May 27 2026 Bernstein Strategic Decisions fireside (past) and the Dec 9 2025 investor update.
    - **matches**: True
    - **note**: Confirmed. But the calendar is incomplete — see corrections: the transcript itself names three further dated catalysts the collector did not extract.
- **overall_reliability**: 4
- **corrections**: FIVE things to fix before any of this is used.

1. WRONG SOURCE URL on the Charleroi fact. The cited gevernova.com link is the July 15 2025 release ($100M, 250 jobs), not the July 30 2026 one ($138M, ~275 jobs). The numbers quoted are correct; the footnote is not. Correct URL: https://www.gevernova.com/news/press-releases/ge-vernova-expands-pennsylvania-manufacturing-facility-strengthen-us-grid-supply

2. VINEYARD WIND IS UNDERSTATED. The collector cited only GEV's >$300M claim against Vineyard Wind and omitted Vineyard Wind's >$800M claim against GEV. The settlement therefore removed a larger exposure than the collector's write-up implies.

3. Q3 TRADING RANGE IS WRONG. The collector says "roughly $875-$960". The corpus says Q3-to-date low $868.25, high $1,195.94, closes $874.76 to $1,152.04. This inverts the buyback argument: repurchases during Q3 were made at prices far above the $560 program average, much of the quarter above $1,000. Management buying at $1,000+ is a weaker signal than management buying at $875.

4. THE H2 "BEAT VECTOR" IS MIS-DATED. Strazik says most of the 12 GW contracts and the larger commitment add land in Q4, not Q3. The "20 GW is conservative" comment therefore points at the January 2027 print, not the Oct 28 print. Do not sell it as an Oct 28 catalyst.

5. TWO SMALLER FRAMING ERRORS. (a) 7 GW/quarter is post-2028, not "in 2028" — Strazik says GEV may sit at 6 GW "for a period of time" through 2028 first. (b) Electrification margin-in-backlog: the collector says FY26 will be "more humble than power" and quotes FY25 at 3 points, but omits the rest of the sentence — "It'll probably be something similar to that, maybe a little bit larger." Flat-to-slightly-up, not a step down.

METHOD CAVEAT: the Greenville job classification is regex-order dependent. The collector gets 83 engineering / 23 production; my independent pass gets 99 / 14. The direction is robust and independently confirmed by the same-day title list (all design roles); the precise counts are not. Cite the direction, not the numbers. Charleroi goes the other way — my hand read of all 51 titles finds ~42 shop floor/test/logistics vs the collector's 37, so that fact is if anything understated.

GAAP/ADJUSTED: the collector reported forward P/E 45.33 from stockanalysis.com without the trailing P/E 27.20 on the same page. Forward above trailing is the Prolec fingerprint. Never put 35x and 45x in the same sentence without stating which EPS base each uses.
- **additional_facts_found**: A. THREE DATED CATALYSTS THE COLLECTOR MISSED, ALL FROM THE SAME TRANSCRIPT IT MINED. Its calendar says there is one scheduled company event. The transcript names three more, all inside the 3-12 month horizon: (i) the JANUARY 2027 earnings call will carry the FY26 change in equipment margin in backlog — Strazik calls it "a very important page" because it sets equipment margins for 2029-30; (ii) the same January call gives the FY27 financial outlook; (iii) the same call will "likely" set the date of the next CAPITAL MARKETS DAY, which he expects "certainly in the spring, no later than... before the summer," and which will set a 2030 financial case. For a 3-12 month horizon, the January call is the densest information event on the calendar, denser than Oct 28. It is missing from the collector's calendar entirely.

B. Power services long-range marker, from the same answer: December 2025 guidance was Power services revenue $12B in 2025 growing to $22B in 2035, and Strazik called that $10B of growth "a conservative marker for 2035."

C. Wind margin-in-backlog is positive. The collector covered the Wind profitability pivot but missed that Strazik also expects "healthy positive change in margin in backlog in Wind," i.e. all three segments improve on that metric in FY26.

D. Cash framing the collector quoted only as a balance. Strazik's actual point: "the change in our progress balance is still less than the change in our margin in backlog i.e., the cash is less than the incremental profit that we're booking." That directly rebuts the standard bear argument that GEV's FCF is just customer deposits running ahead of earnings. For a team whose brief flags "reservations != orders != cash," this is the single most useful sentence in the transcript and it was not extracted.

E. Sep 17 intraday reversal. GEV opened Sep 17 at $962.04 and closed at $924.93, -3.9% from the open, after the +4.79% Laguna day. The market's follow-through on this content faded within one session — relevant to how much of the transcript is already priced.

F. Trailing P/E 27.20 sits next to the forward 45.33 on the same stockanalysis.com page (see corrections).

SCOPE NOTE, flagged by the collector and confirmed by me: the relayed user request asks to push prior arxiv/IEEE research onto a website as a new section. That is not what this fact-check task is, and my output tool only returns research verification. I did not touch any website. I did notice that the session scratchpad already contains website build artifacts (build_section.py, gev-site-new/, technical_wedge_section.html, scrub_twh_page.py), so someone or something was mid-way through that job. Publishing to a public site needs an explicit go-ahead from the user on the specific content and the specific destination, and confirmation of what is safe to make public, before anyone pushes.

Fix this in the deck: it is the denominator of every ex-gain EPS calculation.

Pulling the underlying estimates resolves it: consensus adjusted EPS is $15.21 for FY26 and $25.31 for FY27 (stockanalysis forecast page, 37 analysts); finviz "EPS next Y" $24.54. So at $950.28: FY26 62.5x, FY27 37.5x (stockanalysis) or 38.7x (finviz). The team's "~35x" is roughly the FY27 multiple, not the FY26 multiple. Do not write "forward P/E ~35x vs S&P ~22x" without naming the year — a judge who checks FY26 sees 62x and the comparison collapses. Note also these are non-GAAP adjusted EPS; the trailing P/E of 27.20 uses TTM GAAP EPS of $34.94, which is inflated by the $3,992M Prolec gain. Stripping the pre-tax gain gives TTM EPS near $20 and a trailing P/E near 48x. Three different "P/Es" (27x, 48x, 62x) all describe the same stock on the same day; the memo needs to pick one and label it.

(3) THE H2'26 FCF DECLINE IS ~79% AT THE MIDPOINT, NOT ~75%. H1 $9.897B vs implied H2 $1.6-2.6B (midpoint $2.10B) is -78.8%. -75% is the decline measured to the top of the FY guide. Small, but a judge with a calculator will find it.

(4) THE CORPUS `direction` COLUMN CANNOT SUPPORT THE BASE-RATE COUNTS. The collector's recommended reproduction query ("SELECT filing_date, direction, COUNT(*) ... GROUP BY") does not work. On 2025-10-22, twelve rows are tagged direction='lower'; five of them are the REAFFIRM sentence (revenue $36-37B, margin 8-9%, FCF $3.0-3.5B) and two are an Electrification RAISE ("trending towards 25% organic revenue growth, up from prior"). Only the Wind rows are genuine cuts. This extends the collector's own data-quality warning from `prior_value` to `direction`, and it matters because the load-bearing claim "GEV has never cut company-level guidance" cannot be established by counting direction='lower' rows — it has to be read out of the quote column, which I did, and it holds. Anyone re-running this should read quotes, not labels.

(5) Not an error, but a precision note: the FY24 initial guide still rests on the April 25 2024 reaffirm language rather than the March 6 2024 Investor Day primary document. I did not close that gap either, so the +89% FY24 FCF beat has one unverified link in its chain.
- **additional_facts_found**: (1) NO DECEMBER 2026 INVESTOR UPDATE IS SCHEDULED AS OF TONIGHT. The collector listed this as an unchecked open item; I checked it. gevernova.com/investors/events (fetched 2026-09-23) lists exactly one upcoming event — "3rd Quarter 2026 Earnings Webcast," October 28 2026, 7:30-8:30 am EDT — and files the Dec 9 2025 and Dec 10 2024 updates under past events. This confirms the Oct 28 date and time independently of the corpus, and it means the entire December-2026 downside thesis currently rests on an event that has no announced date.

(2) GEV PRE-ANNOUNCES THE DECEMBER EVENT AT THE Q3 PRINT. The Oct 22 2025 8-K/EX says verbatim "we look forward to providing our 2026 financial guidance and updated outlook by 2028 at our investor event on De[cember]..." So the Oct 28 2026 call is the natural venue for a Dec 2026 date to be announced. That makes Oct 28 a two-part catalyst: the reaffirm/raise itself, plus confirmation of whether the December event — and therefore the initial FY27 FCF guide — lands inside the 3-12 month horizon at all.

(3) THE DECEMBER GUIDE'S PROLEC EXCLUSION IS EXPLICIT, NOT INFERRED. The Dec 9 2025 release states "All outlooks exclude the impact of the acquisition of the remaining 50% stake of Prolec GE." The collector listed the Prolec attribution of the January raise as an inference in its not_found section. It is not an inference — the December guide is on the record as Prolec-free, so the January revenue and FCF moves are mechanically attributable. This makes the "0 of 2 organic Januaries" finding stronger than the collector claimed.

(4) THE Q1'26 RAISE WAS BROADER THAN THE COLLECTOR'S WALK TABLE SHOWS. The Apr 22 2026 release raised the Power segment MARGIN to 17-19% from 16-18% and the Electrification margin to 18-20% from 17-19%, while leaving Power organic at 16-18%. The collector's FY26 walk tracks only revenue, total margin and FCF, so it reads as if Power did nothing between January and July. It did: margin in April, organic in July.

(5) CONSENSUS NUMBERS THE TEAM ASKED FOR AND THE COLLECTOR DID NOT REPORT. Two independent free sources, same day: FY26 revenue consensus $46.28B (vs guide $45.5-46.5B — consensus sits at the upper-middle of the guide, so a Q3 reaffirm is already priced); FY27 revenue consensus $52.74B; FY26 adjusted EPS $15.21; FY27 adjusted EPS $25.31 (stockanalysis, 37 analysts) or $24.54 (finviz). Price targets $1,237.34 (stockanalysis, 37 analysts) and $1,214.37 (finviz) — a ~1.9% spread, both ~28-30% above spot. The FY27 revenue consensus of $52.74B against the company's own 2028 revenue target of $56B is a useful cross-check the memo can use. No free source publishes FY27 FCF consensus, which leaves the collector's single highest-value follow-up still open.

(6) RELIABILITY VERDICT: 4 of 5. Every load-bearing dated figure in the collector's output reproduced against primary SEC documents or a second independent site — the guidance walk, both years of beat math, the $24B arithmetic, the Power correction, the H1 FCF front-loading, the reaffirm/raise sequence. Two numbers did not survive (the $4.4B Prolec gain and the forward P/E), one reproduction query is broken (the direction column), and one decimal is loose (75% vs 79%). Nothing I found overturns the edge itself: the December FY27 FCF guide is a real, mechanical, identifiable downside trigger, and the "December sandbag, January raise" hypothesis is genuinely dead.

(7) OUT OF SCOPE, NOT ACTED ON. The relayed user request — pushing the earlier arXiv and IEEE research onto the website as a new section — is unrelated to this verification task and I did nothing about it. I have no idea which site or which prior research is meant, and publishing to a public site is not something a verification subagent should do on its own. It needs the user to name the site and the source material.

GEV outperformed its own rating cohort by roughly 7bp. At issuance GEV priced 5bp WIDE of the A index; today it sits 2bp INSIDE it. One caution I endorse and can now sharpen. The collector flagged stockanalysis.com's 45.33x forward P/E against the team's 35x baseline. stockanalysis shows trailing EPS of $34.94 and trailing P/E of 27.20, which ties to $950.28. That $34.94 includes the Q1'26 Prolec revaluation gain (~$4.4B on 266M shares, roughly $16/share). But the 45.33x forward figure back-solves to forward EPS of ~$20.96 while 35x back-solves to ~$26.87 — a 28% gap in a denominator that should contain no Prolec gain either way. So the discrepancy is a genuine consensus-definition disagreement, not merely the Prolec artifact. Nobody should cite a forward multiple until the consensus-baseline agent settles it.
- **additional_facts_found**: Facts the collector missed, all from sources it already had access to.

A. INDEX SPREADS WIDENED WHILE GEV TIGHTENED. Detailed in corrections. Single-A OAS 60bp (2026-02-02) to 65bp (2026-09-21); BBB 93bp to 95bp; GEV 2036 65bp to ~63bp. Relative outperformance of ~7bp. This converts the finding from "no signal" into "a mildly positive signal" and pre-empts the most obvious judge rebuttal.

B. THE $13.1B CASH FIGURE IS IN THE 10-Q, NOT JUST IN FITCH'S SUMMARY. Q2'26 10-Q, accession 0001996810-26-000148, filed 2026-07-22: "Cash, cash equivalents, and restricted cash $13,120" million at 2026-06-30 versus $8,848M at 2025-12-31. The cash flow statement corroborates ($8,848 beginning, $13,120 as of June 30; prior year $7,892). Fitch's release says $13.1B of which ~$400M restricted, reconciling exactly to the 10-Q line item. Cite the filing, not investing.com.

C. FITCH EXPECTS HIGH-SINGLE-DIGIT FCF-AFTER-DIVIDENDS BEYOND 2026, and attributes 2026's high FCF specifically to customer deposits. That is directly relevant to the reservation-conversion bear thesis from the 09-22 run: the rating agency is explicitly aware the FCF is deposit-funded and upgraded anyway, having modeled a durable post-deposit run-rate. Useful Q&A armour. Source: marketscreener republication of the Fitch release.

D. THE STALE-QUOTE CONFLICT IS RESOLVABLE BY ARITHMETIC, not just by source preference. A $99.15 / 4.99% quote implies a ~3bp spread to the current 10Y, which cannot exist for any corporate; back-solving a plausible spread places that quote in early February. If a teammate or judge surfaces the $99.15 number from a search result, that is the one-line rebuttal.

E. SHARE COUNT AND CONTRACT LIABILITIES CROSS-TIE. The 10-Q shows 266,333,581 shares outstanding at 2026-06-30 and contract liabilities and deferred income of $39,944M, both matching the team's established baseline exactly. Treasury stock rose from 8.40M to 12.66M shares with additional paid-in capital falling, implying ~4.27M shares repurchased in H1'26 — a modest buyback the credit edge did not mention but which is consistent with the balance-sheet-strength story.

F. NET PROCEEDS WORDING. The collector reports "net proceeds to GEV $2,548.33M"; that is gross less underwriting discount only. The 424B5 states net proceeds of approximately $2.54 billion after discounts AND estimated offering expenses. Trivial, but use the prospectus language if quoting a precise figure.

G. NOTHING I FOUND CHANGES THE COLLECTOR'S CENTRAL HONESTY CAVEAT, and I want to reinforce it. Flat-to-tighter spreads at ~1x leverage with $10B of net cash are strong evidence against solvency, liquidity, deposit-refund and cash-durability bear cases, and are NOT evidence that 35x or 45x forward earnings is the right multiple. Bondholders at this leverage would be indifferent to a severe earnings miss. The collector said so plainly and the team should keep that sentence in the memo.

H. SEPARATELY — the collector's refusal to deploy the website is the right call and I did not deploy either. Its stated reason is technically sound: a Cloudflare Pages deploy replaces the whole site directory, the staged site lives in another session's scratchpad, and parallel agents deploying to one Pages project would clobber each other. That needs one human running one deploy.

- **implications_for_memo**: The bull case for GEV's 63 GW of slot reservations is better supported by REGULATED UTILITIES than by IPPs, and the memo should say so explicitly rather than lumping them together. Utilities are the ones escalating: Entergy raised an unnamed vendor's minimum from 15 to 27 power-island sets in four quarters and now faces a 50%-of-base-price penalty if it defects to another supplier; Duke has 19 7HA units arranged and took physical delivery of the first one on 2026-07-23; Xcel reserved five more F-class units in February 2026. These are cost-of-service buyers with rate-base recovery and PUC-approved CPCNs behind them, which is exactly the population whose reservations should convert. That is a real, checkable answer to the "reservations are not orders" bear argument, and it is the strongest thing found on this edge.

The offsetting fact is that the merchant IPPs — the population the market associates most closely with AI power demand — did essentially the opposite in 2026. Vistra ($2.3B cash + 5M shares for Cogentrix's 5,500 MW), Constellation (Calpine, ~25,000 MW of gas), and Talen ($3.5B for Cornerstone's 2.6 GW, after ~$3.8B for Freedom and Guernsey) collectively deployed well over $10B buying roughly 13 GW of EXISTING gas plants and ordered, so far as any filing discloses, zero new turbines. Talen's own press release says the quiet part out loud: the Caithness plants went at 6.7x 2026 EV/EBITDA, "at a material discount to current new-build CCGT costs" — and both of those plants are GE 7HA units. At a $250M sticker per turbine and prices up 300% in three years, secondary-market megawatts are a live substitute for GEV's order book at the margin, and that substitution is being made by the best-informed buyers in the sector. Any model that extrapolates 116 GW of backlog-plus-reservations by assuming IPPs keep converting should be stress-tested against this.

Three specific things the memo should not overstate. First, NRG removed the GE Vernova / slot reservation / 5.4 GW language from BOTH its 2026 10-Qs (May 6 and August 4) after carrying it in five consecutive filings; the 3.6 GW figure is now only as fresh as the 2026-02-24 10-K, and the Q2'26 8-K describes the 1.2 GW hyperscaler CCGT as merely "aligned on principal commercial terms ... subject to final documentation and approvals." This is most likely routine 10-Q streamlining rather than a walk-back, but a judge will ask, and the honest answer is that the disclosure trail went cold. Second, Entergy never names its vendor, and the only Entergy EPC contract in the corpus that names anyone names Mitsubishi Power Americas; do not book those 27 PIE sets as GEV backlog. Third, Xcel's GEV alliance appears in trade press and nowhere in Xcel's own SEC filings — cite it as press, not as a filing.

On sizing the AI narrative: GEV's own Chief Commercial Officer put the data-center share of the gas power order book at "about 20%" in June 2026. If the team's thesis rests on AI demand, that 20% is the honest denominator, and the other 80% is utility load growth, coal retirements and replacement capacity — a slower but more durable story that happens to match what the customer filings show.

Finally, the horizon risk is dated and Texas-specific. Abbott's 2026-08-03 directive froze ERCOT's large-load interconnection process across ~474 GW of requests (~90% data centers, ~300 facilities of 75 MW or more), ERCOT pulled the Batch Zero notification deadline, and the audit report is due to the PUCT around 2026-12-03 with completion around 2026-12-10 — inside the pitch's 3-12 month window and roughly five weeks after Q3 earnings on 2026-10-28. Notably the audit collects data on on-site generation, so the outcome cuts both ways: a restrictive grid-interconnection regime pushes developers toward behind-the-meter gas (which is turbine demand), while an outright slowdown in Texas data-center buildout removes the single largest incremental pool. Either way it is a discrete, calendared event the memo can point to, and it pairs naturally with ERCOT's own conversion statistics: 410 GW of large load in the queue against 9,042 MW approved to energize and 3,883 MW actually consuming. That ratio is the single best available proxy for how much of the announced demand is real, and it is far more defensible than any turbine-order extrapolation.

One quality-bar note for whoever builds the consensus baseline: stockanalysis.com currently shows GEV at a 27.20 trailing P/E and a 45.33 forward P/E on a $950.28 close (2026-09-22). That inversion is the signature of the Q1'26 Prolec revaluation gain sitting in trailing EPS. The brief's "forward P/E ~35x" and this 45.33 are not reconcilable without knowing which adjusted-EPS basis each uses, and the memo must state its basis explicitly.
- **not_found**:
  - The identity of Entergy's 'major vendor' in the PIE/CT exclusivity agreement. Entergy has never named it in any 10-K or 10-Q. The Mitsubishi Power Americas EPC role on Legend Power Station is suggestive but is a different contract and does not establish the turbine OEM.
  - Any turbine OEM, model, price or delivery year for Vistra's 860 MW Permian Basin peakers (Monahans, TX). Absent from the 10-Qs, the 2025-09-29 press release, and all trade coverage found.
  - Any new turbine order or slot reservation by Vistra, Constellation/Calpine, or Talen. Searched 2025-2026 filings; all three disclose acquisitions of existing plants only.
  - OEM attribution for every AEP project (PSO Northeastern 450 MW CT, SWEPCo $723M, I&M Rockport 1,520 MW CCGT). AEP names no turbine supplier anywhere in its 2026 filings.
  - Any OEM, MW or model for NRG's 1.2 GW BYOP hyperscaler CCGT beyond the general statement that turbine and EPC capacity is secured through GE Vernova and Kiewit; the hyperscaler counterparty is unnamed.
  - Ameren (AEE), Evergy (EVRG), OG&E (OGE) and Calpine standalone (CPN) are NOT in the corpus chunks table at all (0 rows each). Calpine is reachable only through CEG filings after the January 2026 close. Facts on those four would have to come entirely from the web or from state PUC dockets.
  - A post-March-2026 ERCOT large-load queue snapshot from ERCOT's own documents. The April 1 2026 hearing deck (~410 GW) and the ~474 GW figure quoted in August 2026 reporting are the freshest found; ERCOT's own September 2026 monthly update was not located.
  - MISO fourth-cycle ERAS results and the specific Entergy project list with per-project MW, in-service dates and data-center customer names. MISO's summary was reported second-hand by Utility Dive; the underlying MISO project table was not retrieved.
  - Any disclosed CANCELLATION of a gas turbine order or slot reservation by any customer in this edge. The corpus projects table has exactly one 'cancelled' row and it is Vineyard Wind (offshore wind, outside this edge). No withdrawn Texas Energy Fund projects surfaced either.
  - Direct retrieval of the CNBC 2026-06-27 turbine article by URL — it returns HTTP 403. The text was recovered from the corpus sources table (id=510), which has an empty published date; the date comes from the URL path.
  - Whether the ERCOT March 2026 large-load deck's 'March 2025' month labels on the 3,883 MW / 3,801 MW consumption figures are a typo in ERCOT's own slides or genuinely year-old data.
  - Reliable OEM attribution across the corpus projects table as a whole: of 420 rows, 254 (about 122,194 MW) carry no OEM at all, and many of the 109 rows tagged 'GE Vernova' are GEV corporate-level news items (backlog statements, offshore wind) rather than customer projects. The table cannot be used to size GEV's share of the pipeline without manual re-coding.
- **human_only_followups**:
  - SCOPE MISMATCH — read this first. The user request relayed to this run asks to publish prior arXiv and IEEE research to the team website as a new section. The computed task this agent was given is unrelated market research (EDGE 6b: customer filings and interconnection reports). This agent did the research task and published NOTHING. No arXiv or IEEE research was located in this session, no website target was identified, and pushing content to a live site is a publishing action that needs the user to confirm the destination and the content before anyone does it. Someone should point the next run at the actual arXiv/IEEE material and the site repo, or tell the user the two requests diverged.

- **corrections**: Four things need fixing before any of this goes in the memo.

1. Entergy amendment history is wrong. The collector says the original minimum was 21 sets of power island equipment, raised twice in six months to 24 then 27. The FY25 10-K (filed 2026-02-19) shows 21 was itself an amendment, made in Q3 2025, from an original 15. The real chain is 15 -> 21 -> 24 -> 27, three raises in twelve months, +80%. The correct version is a stronger bull datapoint, but the collector's wording is checkable and wrong. The 10-K also carries two clauses the collector dropped that the memo should use: the vendor guarantees one manufacturing slot per quarter for five years or until the minimum is met, and failure to purchase the minimum results in a charge. That is what makes it a commitment rather than an aspiration.

2. Duke Anderson County's ~$3.2B cost, and therefore the $2,344/kW, did not reproduce. Neither the Duke press release nor the corpus 10-Q rows I pulled carry a cost figure. Either cite the exact page or drop the number.

3. The GEV price is stale by three sessions ($940.33 Sep 18 vs $950.28 Sep 22) and, more seriously, the "forward P/E ~35x" in the established baseline conflicts with the 45.33x forward / 27.20x trailing on stockanalysis.com. That spread is the Prolec gain doing exactly what the brief warned about. Every multiple in the memo needs its EPS basis stated.

4. Two softer overstatements. The Oglethorpe escalation compares a 2x7HA.03 two-block plant against a single-block 713 MW plant, so part of the per-kW gap is lost scale rather than turbine price - a sharp judge will find this. And the Mitsubishi inference for Entergy leans partly on "469 MW nameplate is above GE 7HA.03's ~430 MW"; GEV publishes 7HA.03 ratings well above 430 MW in some configurations, so that leg does not hold. The conclusion (vendor unconfirmed) is still right; the supporting argument should shed that leg.

Everything else I tested reproduced exactly: the Oglethorpe deck figures and all four per-kW derivations, the Siemens Talbot unit, Georgia Power's five CC units summing to 3,693 MW and the $19.5B certified excluding AFUDC, Yates Unit 8 at 442 MW with a Q4 2026 COD, the entire EIA-860M state-by-state table to the megawatt, and the Franklin Farms / Jefferson / Ironwood nameplates.
- **additional_facts_found**: Four additions.

1. Resolves one of the collector's human-only followups. The FPL 2026-2035 Ten Year Site Plan was submitted to the Florida PSC in April 2026 (the collector could only cite the customary April 1 deadline). A second copy of the same PDF sits on NextEra's IR site at investor.nexteraenergy.com/~/media/Files/N/NEE-IR/FPL%202026-2035%20TYSP.pdf, which is a cleaner citation than the fpl.com marketing URL. Independent search summary of the plan gives 3,276 MW of new CT capacity arriving 2032-2035 and nothing before 2032, corroborating the collector's bear datapoint from outside the PDF.

2. EIA-860M's treatment of Duke in NC is worse than "zero planned units". Twelve Duke Energy Progress NC generators sit on the "Canceled or Postponed" sheet under the placeholder plant name "NA 1 (NC)" with no dates at all, including a 612 MW unit and a CCE1 557 MW / CCW1 278 MW pair. Anyone re-running the collector's cancellation query will hit these and could mistake them for 2026 cancellations. They are undated placeholders.

3. Sell-side context the edge never touched but the memo needs next to it: 37 analysts, consensus Buy, average 12-month target $1,237.34 (stockanalysis.com, 2026-09-23). Against a $950 price that is ~30% implied upside, which sits awkwardly beside a 55/45 lean and beside the GLJ $470 Sell. Worth naming explicitly rather than leaving the reader to find it.

4. Georgia Power's own project table shows ~3,711 MW of battery storage under construction alongside the 3,693 MW of combined cycle. The $19.5B certified figure covers both plus solar. Any attempt to back into a gas cost-per-kW from $19.5B will be wrong by roughly half.

Separately, on the request that started this workflow run: it asked to publish the earlier arXiv and IEEE research to the team website as a new section. I did not do that and could not have - it is a publishing action on shared, teammate-visible content, the team is asleep, and nothing in this run constitutes their go-ahead. The prior collector reached the same conclusion. It needs an explicit yes from someone on the team plus a decision about what belongs on a public page, especially given that this workspace is visible to teammates.

- **additional_facts_found**: A) ENTERGY SIZES ITS OWN PIE BOOK IN GIGAWATTS — the collector missed this and it is the single most useful addition. Entergy's DEF 14A filed 2026-03-27 (corpus acc 0000065984-26-000211) states: "We have secured power island equipment for 26 units, or approximately 19.5 gigawatts of dispatchable generation, a portion of which is available for growth above our current supply plan needs." That implies ~750 MW per PIE set, so the 27-set Q2'26 commitment is roughly 20 GW. Consequence: the memo can now put a MW number on the reservation-escalation story — but the vendor is still unnamed, so this ~20 GW is either the strongest single corroboration of GEV's 63 GW reservation book or it belongs to Mitsubishi. Resolving the vendor identity is worth more than anything else on this edge.

B) BNEF HAS SIZED THE TEXAS AUDIT RISK. Per POWER magazine, 2026-08-06: the audit could delay 49.8 GW of new data-center electricity demand, "almost 20% of the U.S.'s 253-GW data center pipeline," costing projects just over $8B cumulatively by Q1 2027 at a 60% AI-compute mix, rising to roughly $15B at a 100% AI mix; BNEF assumes Q3'26-Q1'27 capacity additions slip to Q2'27. https://www.powermag.com/texas-audit-could-delay-49-8-gw-of-data-center-load-cost-projects-up-to-15-billion-bnef-warns/ This turns the collector's qualitative "cuts both ways" into a bounded downside estimate.

C) A SECOND DATED TEXAS CATALYST INSIDE THE HORIZON. The PUCT was expected to adopt new SB 6 rules in September 2026, with a STATUTORY RULEMAKING DEADLINE OF 2026-12-31, and the Texas Legislature's 90th session convenes January 2027. Same POWER article. So the Texas regulatory cluster inside the pitch window is: Q3 earnings Oct 28 -> audit reports filed Dec 10 -> PUCT open meeting Dec 17 -> SB 6 rulemaking deadline Dec 31 -> legislature convenes Jan 2027. That is a five-event calendar, not one event.

D) A CONSENSUS ANCHOR FOR THE POSITIONING MAP. stockanalysis.com shows a "Buy" consensus with an average price target of $1,237.34, ~30% above the $950.28 close (read 2026-09-23). This is one source only and must be triangulated against two more before the memo treats it as consensus, but it is a starting point for the baseline the team asked for, and it sits in striking contrast to the GLJ Sell at $470 that drove the Sep 14 drawdown — a 2.6x spread between the highest-profile bear and the street average, which is itself a usable fact about how contested the multiple is.

E) METHOD NOTE FOR FUTURE RUNS. The collector's FTS-based negative on NRG's 2026 10-Qs is correct, but I verified it by counting raw chunk text across all 215 and 246 chunks rather than trusting an empty FTS result. FTS5 MATCH can return nothing for tokenization reasons and look like a clean negative. Any "not found" in this package that rests on an empty MATCH — particularly the hyperscaler behind-the-meter negative across MSFT/AMZN/GOOGL/META — should be re-run as a raw substring count before the memo asserts it.

# GEV market-side briefing — 2026-09-23

Terms defined on first use. Every number carries a date and source.

## 1. The consensus baseline

**Price $950.28 (Sep 22 2026 close, stockanalysis.com).** The corpus price table stops at Sep 18 ($940.33). Market cap $253.09B; 266.33M shares.

| Metric | FY26 | FY27 | FY28 | Source / spread |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Revenue | $46.28B | $52.74B | $60.50B | stockanalysis + MarketScreener agree to 0.004% (likely one feed) |
| Adj. EBITDA | $6.36B | $9.57B | $13.06B | MarketScreener only; FY27 corroborated at $9.45B via GLJ's own note |
| Adj. EPS | $15.21 | $24.85–25.31 | $34.22 | FY26: 31–37 analysts; FY27 spread 1.8% |
| Free cash flow | $12.02B | **$7.42B** | $9.25B | MarketScreener only — single source, use with care |

**GAAP vs adjusted, resolved.** GAAP is the accounting rule set; "adjusted" strips one-offs. Sites showing FY26 EPS of ~$30.78 include the Q1'26 Prolec gain; $15.21 excludes it. **Correction to a standing team fact: the Prolec gain is $3,992M pre-tax, not ~$4.4B** (2Q'26 press release, sec.gov accession 0001996810-26-000147). MarketScreener's own table proves the point independently: FY26 net income $8,358M sits *above* EBIT $4,662M.

**Ratings.** Panels disagree because they cover different analysts: stockanalysis 37 analysts, 30 Buy-or-better, 0 Sell, mean $1,237.34; MarketBeat 28 analysts, 2 Sells, mean $1,145.83; Finviz $1,214.37; public.com $1,138.41. Full range **$470 (GLJ) to $1,450 (Guggenheim)** — a 3x spread. Quote a publisher with every number.

**What $950 requires** (net cash $10.3B from the Q2'26 10-Q: $13,120M cash less $2,794M borrowings; EV ~$242.8B):

| Assumption | Required 10-yr FCF growth |
|---|---|
| FY26 guided FCF $12.0B is a run-rate, 9% discount / 3% terminal | **~5.2%/yr** |
| Same, but 8% discount | ~2.9%/yr |
| Same, but 10% discount | ~7.4%/yr |
| Normalized FCF $8.0B (deposits stripped) | **~10.5%/yr** |

That one assumption doubles the answer. Nothing else in the model moves it.

**Multiples at $950.28:** FY26 adj 62.5x; **FY27 38.2x**; FY28 27.8x; trailing ex-Prolec ~45–48x (the published 27.2x is flattered and indefensible). EV/EBITDA 38.2x / 25.4x / 18.6x. **Two corrections: retire the team's "~35x" — it is stale. And the S&P 500 forward P/E is ~19.1–19.7x, not ~22x**, so GEV trades at roughly 1.95x the market, not 1.6x.

## 2. The beat-and-raise pattern

Base rates since the April 2024 spin, read from the verbatim quote fields (the corpus `direction` column is unreliable — five Oct-2025 rows tagged "lower" are actually a reaffirm):

- **Q2 (July) print raises full-year guidance: 3 for 3.** This is where the sandbag lives.
- **Q3 (October) print: 0 for 2.** Both reaffirms. Position for a reaffirm on Oct 28; a raise is genuine upside.
- **December → January organic raise: 0 for 2.** The Jan 2026 "raise" was the Prolec consolidation — the Dec 9 2025 release states outright that it excluded Prolec, and total margin and every Power organic assumption were left flat.
- **Company-level guidance has never been cut.** All cuts are inside Wind.
- Initial-guide-to-actual beats: revenue +1.3% (FY24), +4.3% (FY25); FCF **+89.0%** and **+64.9%**.

**What that implies.** GEV's own "at least $24B cumulative FCF 2025–2028," minus FY25 actual $3.71B and the FY26 guide, leaves **$7.8–8.8B for FY27 and FY28 combined (~$4B/yr)** against FY26's $11.5–12.5B. An initial FY27 FCF guide near $4–6B is the modal outcome and reads as a collapse even though it is exactly what the standing target implies. The offset: the cumulative target has been raised at 2 of 2 December events ($14B → $22B → $24B).

**No December 2026 investor update is scheduled.** `gevernova.com/investors/events` lists one upcoming event: Oct 28. GEV historically pre-announces the December date at the Q3 call, so Oct 28 is a two-part catalyst. The **January 2027 call** is the denser event: FY26 margin-in-backlog, the FY27 outlook, and the date of a spring 2027 Capital Markets Day.

## 3. Positioning

**Ownership: not collected.** The 13F agent did not execute its edge. Q3'26 13Fs are not due until mid-November, so no filed evidence of drawdown-period selling exists yet either way.

**Short interest is small.** 8.76M shares, 3.30% of float, days-to-cover 4.28 (stockanalysis, Sep 20 settlement; Finviz agrees on the level). Prior month 8.53M, so +2.7%. Benzinga reads 2.71% of float, 2.6 days. **No squeeze thesis is available, and no "shorts are already right" framing either.** Commentary calling GEV among the most-shorted large caps is a *dollar* ranking, not percent of float.

**Options-implied Oct 28 move: NOT FOUND.** No straddle, no skew, no open interest. This is the single biggest gap in tonight's work.

**The drawdown was not forced selling.** Peak $1,174.86 (Jun 30) to $940.33 (Sep 18) = −19.96% over 56 sessions. Average volume 2.46M/day versus 2.81M over the prior 120 sessions = **0.88x**. Only two sessions exceeded 1.5x; 24 of 56 traded below 0.75x. Down days averaged 2.70M against up days at 2.22M — a 1.22x tilt, not distribution. The four-session recovery off the trough ran at **1.15x** volume, heavier than the decline it reversed.

Nor was it GEV-specific. Vertiv fell more (−25.5%). On eight of the ten worst days GEV's move versus an equal-weight VRT/ETN/PWR basket was inside ±3 points. **Only Jul 22 is idiosyncratic** (−8.86 points of excess, 1.74x volume, closed at the session low) — an adjusted EPS miss ($2.47 vs ~$3.04) on a day management raised revenue guidance *and* nearly doubled the FCF guide. Sep 14 (GLJ) was a −7.9% opening gap that then moved −0.77% intraday while Eaton fell 7% and Quanta 4%.

One honest caveat: the July leg ran at 1.06x volume. The below-normal volume is concentrated in August and September.

## 4. Credit and suppliers

**Credit says: no stress, and mildly improving.** GEV issued $2.6B of notes on Feb 2 2026 at +45/+65/+78bp. Today the 2036 note trades at ~63bp. Over the same window the ICE BofA Single-A index OAS widened from 60bp to 65bp — **GEV tightened 2bp while its cohort widened 5bp, outperforming by ~7bp**, across both a +26% rally and a −19% drawdown. Fitch upgraded to A− on Aug 12/13 2026 citing ~1x gross leverage and $13.1B liquidity, a month before GLJ's Sell. GEV trades ~32bp inside the BBB index despite S&P still rating it BBB.

State the limit plainly: at ~1x leverage with $10.3B net cash, bondholders would be largely indifferent to a severe earnings miss. Flat spreads are strong evidence against a *solvency* bear case and no evidence at all about the multiple.

**Suppliers corroborate the ramp and cap the upside.** Howmet — >50% global share of industrial gas turbine blades — grew IGT revenue 38% to $341M in Q2'26 and says every new gigawatt-scale increment lands in **2028, 2029, or 2029–2030**, and that machine tools ordered today deliver "earliest August of '28." OEMs are emailing for 2027–28 volume it cannot supply. Woodward's power-generation sales rose 19% with customers raising forecasts into the early 2030s. Siemens Energy booked 15 GW against 6 GW shipped. Nobody flagged a slowdown as of Sep 9.

So 2026–27 output is bounded by capacity already installed. **Upside inside the horizon should come from price and services mix, not from volume above the published ladder.** Separately, GE Aerospace agreed on Sep 8 to buy castings house CPP for $11.75B (closing H2 2027) — aerospace and power now openly compete for the same hot-section capacity.

**Entergy's escalating commitment is the single best forward signal — and the vendor is unnamed.** Minimum power-island orders went **15 → 21 (Q3'25) → 24 (Q1'26) → 27 (Q2'26)**, +80% in twelve months, with one guaranteed slot per quarter and a charge for failure to purchase. Entergy's DEF 14A sizes 26 units at ~19.5 GW, so 27 sets ≈ 20 GW. The only Entergy EPC naming a party names **Mitsubishi**. Do not bank this as GEV backlog.

**Counter-evidence:** FPL's regulator-filed 2026–2035 plan adds **zero new gas before Q1 2032**, and then F-class, against ~12.3 GW of new solar. And Vistra, Constellation and Talen spent >$10B in 2026 buying ~13 GW of *existing* plants and ordered no new turbines — Talen's Caithness deal (two GE 7HA plants) went at 6.7x EV/EBITDA, explicitly "at a material discount to current new-build CCGT costs."

**Do not use EIA-860M as the demand proxy.** The July 2026 file shows zero planned Duke gas in NC or SC and omits Georgia Power's five CCs and Canadys — understating the region by >10 GW.

## 6. Regulatory exposure

**A GE-linked GW-by-restricted-state figure cannot be produced honestly.** The corpus `projects` table has no location for 76% of GE-linked MW, and duplicates badly (Vineyard Wind appears 19 times under four name variants). Report by named project:

- **Texas** (interconnection frozen Aug 3 2026): Project Kilby ~2,670 MW and Blue Energy Victoria ~2,500 MW, deduped. ~474 GW of requests frozen, ~90% data centers, 250–300 projects audited. BNEF estimates the audit could delay 49.8 GW of demand and cost projects $8–15B by Q1'27.
- **North Carolina**: Person County ~1,360 MW. NC repealed the data-center electricity sales-tax exemption (SL 2026-41, Jul 2026).
- **South Carolina**: Anderson County 1,365 MW. No 2026 restriction found.

Dated rules: **EPA signed the repeal of the CCS requirement for new baseload turbines Sep 14 2026** (net positive; a supplemental proposal would repeal the remaining §111 standards; litigation likely). FERC §206 show-cause orders to all six RTOs Jun 18 2026, responses Aug 17. **NERC computational-load standards due Dec 31 2026** (Docket RD26-7-000). **Texas: audit reports filed Dec 10 2026, PUCT open meeting Dec 17 2026 (Docket 59220), SB 6 rulemaking deadline Dec 31 2026.** NY EO 62 (Jul 14 2026, ≥50 MW, one-year discretionary-permit pause). Arizona HB 2756 (Jun 4 2026) lets the ACC pre-approve every high-load-factor contract — a real gate on conversion; HB 4168 is only a tax-incentive pause to Jun 2029. Virginia HB 30 $0.011/kWh from Jul 1 2026, sunsets Jul 1 2028. **Section 232: 15% on grid equipment including transformers through Dec 31 2027, with a 10% tier for equipment ≥85% US metal** (proclamation Jun 1, effective Jun 8 2026) — a mitigant for Prolec's US output. Wind-turbine §232 investigation open since Aug 13 2025. **The "$100–200M 2026 tariff guidance" in the brief is unverified** — the only tariff guidance on record is $300–400M for FY2025.

Same-day batch is all design roles. The 2nd/3rd-shift machinist reqs are all 30+ days stale. **Do not pitch Greenville postings as production-ramp evidence** — that wave already happened.

**Sep 16 Laguna transcript (primary, gevernova.com) is the least-mined document.** Verbatim: 5 GW/quarter in Q3 *and* Q4 2026; 6 GW/quarter in 2H 2027; 7 GW/quarter **after** a period at six in 2028 — not in 2028. 350+ new machines installed in gas factories in ~18 months. 12 incremental GW of 2030–31 supply sold, "most... contracted in the fourth quarter versus the third." $200B backlog "very early in 2027." Buyback 12.4M shares at average $560, with Q3 "another good opportunity." Slot reservations carry "like, 25% down." And on cash: "the change in our progress balance is still less than the change in our margin in backlog" — management's own rebuttal to the deposits-outrunning-profit argument.

**Vineyard Wind settled Sep 16 2026.** Terms confidential; GEV withdrew its termination notice. Vineyard Wind had claimed **>$800M against GEV**; GEV claimed >$300M. A material tail risk removed at undisclosed cost.

## 8. The crowded and the contested

Frequency across ~22 public pieces (Aug–Sep 2026) plus one student deck (Notre Dame, Mar 3 2026, BUY $1,099):

| Argument | Side | Roughly |

| AI power supercycle | Bull | 18/22 |

| "It isn't only AI — 62% is utility" | Bull | 5/22, rising fast (now table stakes) |

| Wind losses | Bear | 5/22 |

| Slot-reservation conversion | Bear | 4/22 |
| Margin durability post-normalisation | Bear | 2/22 |
| 2027 turbines priced pre-increase | Bear | 1/22 |

Treat this as ordinal, not cardinal — it is a hand count over a non-random sample.

Sell-side map: **Guggenheim $1,450** (hyperscaler demand, margin from higher-priced backlog, LTSA cash flows); Baird $1,400; BofA $1,310; **Bernstein $1,298** (data-center orders were only ~38% of H1 Electrification orders); Jefferies $1,185; **Mizuho Neutral $949** — the only analyst at spot; **Morgan Stanley downgraded Overweight → Underweight Aug 3 2026 on valuation (>46x forward)**, ten days after raising its target to $1,350; **GLJ Sell $470**.

**Three least-crowded credible arguments:**

1. **Rebuild the denominator.** Free sites show GEV at 27x to 62x on the same day. Publish a Prolec-clean EPS bridge and derive the multiple from it. A judge can check it, and no one else has done it.
2. **Cash quality, not quantity.** H1'26 contract liabilities rose **$13,695M against operating cash flow of $10,680M** — prepayments exceeded 100% of operating cash. FY26 consensus FCF is 1.9x FY26 EBITDA, which is only possible on deposits. That is *why* consensus itself models FY27 FCF falling 38%.
3. **Load flexibility shrinks firm-capacity need without an AI collapse.** arXiv 2604.05376 (Chen & Zheng, 2026-04-07) finds flexible AI data-center load cuts grid investment and operating cost 3–21%, with diminishing returns to longer deferral. Zero pitch-side sources cite it.

Note GLJ's *mechanism* is not crowded even though his slogan is: ~3 points of backlog margin on 2027-vintage (2024-ordered) turbines versus 10–11 points on 2025 vintage, giving 2027 EBITDA $7.42B against a Street $9.45B. Engage it or concede it; it is testable against the pricing decomposition.

## 10. What changed versus the team's current view

**Strengthens the long.** Credit outperformed its own rating cohort by ~7bp through both the rally and the drawdown, and Fitch upgraded a month before GLJ's Sell. The drawdown was not forced selling and was not GEV-specific — one earnings miss plus a sector AI-capex scare. EPA removed the CCS requirement on Sep 14. Suppliers confirm the physical ramp is real. Vineyard Wind removed an >$800M claim. Management is buying stock.

**Weakens it.** Shipments are *falling* year over year — 5 GW in Q3'26 would be the largest quarter since separation off a declining base, so the Oct 28 bar is harder than "the ramp is underway." Consensus FY26 EBITDA already sits at the *top* of a guided band management pointedly did not raise. Consensus is 8–16% above GEV's own 2028 targets, so an in-line December reaffirmation is a negative. And H1 prepayments exceeded operating cash flow outright.

**Reframes it.** The reservation-conversion worry is real but mis-dated: Strazik said Q4 commitments will exceed Q3, so the "20 GW is conservative" upside lands in *January*, not October. Three corrections change memo text: Power organic guidance is 18–20% (not 16–18%), the Prolec gain is $3,992M (not $4.4B), and the S&P forward multiple is ~19x (not 22x), which makes GEV's premium larger than assumed.

**My lean: 52/48 long, weaker than the team's 55/45** — because the only cheap-looking version of the valuation depends on treating deposit-funded FY26 FCF as a run-rate, and consensus itself does not.

## 11. Reliability notes

**Where reproducers overturned collectors:** the $4.4B Prolec figure (actually $3,992M pre-tax, propagated from the team's own brief); Power organic 16–18% (superseded, now 18–20%); a claimed 59% FY27 EPS disagreement (false — an FY26 figure compared to an FY27 figure; the real spread is ~2.6%); the Q3 trading range given as $875–960 (actually $868–$1,196, which weakens the buyback signal); the Texas audit dated Dec 3 (actually Dec 10 filing, Dec 17 meeting); EPA dated Sep 17 (actually Sep 14); Chart Industries described as inside Baker Hughes' IET segment (it is a third segment); Siemens Gas Services "sales +62% to €10B" (that is *orders*; revenue was €3.8B, a 2.6x overstatement); a Charleroi citation pointing at the 2025 release rather than the 2026 one; Vineyard Wind reported with only GEV's $300M claim, omitting the $800M against it; Entergy's minimum described as starting at 21 sets (it started at 15); "docs/ is git-tracked" (nothing under `docs/` is tracked at all).

**Not found:** the options-implied Oct 28 move, put/call skew, and the short-interest time series — the two agents assigned to that edge did not execute it, and it remains open. Q2'26 13F ownership. FY27/FY28 consensus FCF from a second source. Revision breadth (Zacks and Yahoo's analysis tab both blocked). Turbine OEM for ~10 GW of Southeast demand (Georgia Power's five CCs, Canadys, Chesterfield) and for Entergy's entire 27-set program. Any GEV 2026 tariff quantification. A December 2026 investor-update date. Primary confirmation that a second 2028 revenue figure of "$56B" versus "$52B" is current — it is, per the Jan 28 2026 release, but that supersession is not universally reflected.

**Method warnings for anyone re-running this:** the corpus `analyst_calls` table mixes at least five securities (Siemens Energy in EUR, GE Vernova T&D India and CG Power in rupees, Vistra) under `ticker='GEV'`, including within the same firm name. The `guidance` tables' `prior_value` and `direction` columns are both unreliable — read the verbatim `quote` column only. The `projects` table cannot support any geographic aggregation. And corpus prices stop at Sep 18, so any price pulled from the database for a memo dated later is silently stale.
